THE PRIZE COUNTER SCREEN (`shared/prizes.js` = `Arcade.Prizes.open({onClose})/close()/state()/openCard(key)` + `shared/prizes.css`, loaded by the floor page; tokens `--pz-*` in theme.css): an overlay (`#prizes`, `UI.layer` min 72, trap, Esc) in an original retro prize-counter look: neon PRIZES (a slow glow), the wallet, TICKET the counter bot (a boxy robot, a token-slot mouth, one antenna bulb; one line per opening from a short list, rotating per session; optional voice `prize-hello`, silent until recorded) with a counter bell, THE TOKEN COUNTER machine (the star sources in a `<details>`, "12 new ★ = 60 tokens", TURN IN: stars drop in, coins spill into the tray, the balance counts up, `prize-tokens` (falls back to `quest-tokens`); no button with 0 new: "Earn stars in any game, then come back!"), the PRIZE OF THE WEEK spotlight ("PRIZE OF THE WEEK · 20 % OFF", the old price crossed out, "New prize in 3 days"; owned: "You got this week's prize!"), then the pegboard wall with shelves by full price: 400+ hanging from a rail at the top, 300–399 upper, 200–299 lower, the Manor Collection behind purple glass, 50–199 in the glass case at the front; each prize = `LockerUI.picOf` (the Locker's own thumbnail) + a paper price tag on a string, OWNED, ★ on the wish. TRY-ON: a tap opens a card (`#pzCard`, UI.layer min 76) with the avatar wearing it (live preview), name, price, BUY (yellow; disabled "Need 40 more tokens"; + a line when new stars are waiting), ☆ WISH (one wish, `gameData('prizes').wish`), CLOSE; BUY → `UI.confirm` "Buy the Jetpack for 300 tokens?" → `Tokens.buy` + `prize-win` (falls back to `skin-unlocked`) → WEAR IT NOW / KEEP SHOPPING. ENTER A CODE (Band Ninja belt codes: `BandNinja.redeem` + `Skins.catchUp`, the same as the booth's). Keys: arrows move between prizes (and the spotlight) by position, Enter opens, Esc closes the card then the counter; every control ≥ 44 px. Phones (≤ 760 px): one column, the machine + spotlight (`.pz-top`) sticky on top while the shelves scroll. Reduced motion or MOTION off (`.pz-still`): nothing moves (the count-up jumps). WHERE IT OPENS: the lobby's PRIZE COUNTER sign (lobby.js `prizeSign`: the last `.zsign`, amber, same size and flicker; the balance + "n new ★ to turn in"; the WISH bar "Wish: Jetpack · 180 / 300" with a lighter part for the stars waiting ("+60 waiting at the counter"), "You can get your wish! 🎟" with a gentle pulse once affordable; redrawn on `arcade:tokens`), the avatar badge's menu ("🎟 142 tokens · Prize Counter": over the floor, or from a game page `index.html?prizes`, which arcade.js opens once and drops from the address), and the zone view's counter: 3D = a textured counter + lit PRIZES sign on the back wall (arcade3d.js, layer 1, drawn once) with a see-through `.prize3d` button placed over it (hidden when off screen or < 44 px), 2D = `.prize2d` in the aisle's top left (hidden ≤ 700 px). RESULTS: every `UI.results.show` adds "+3 ★ = 15 tokens at the Prize Counter" (`#resTokens`, text only) when stars were earned since the page opened or the last results screen (`Tokens.resultLine`). Tests: tests/prize-counter.spec.js.
- PORTRAIT IMAGES (Mat's artwork, `shared/portraits/`, mapping + how-to in its README.md): show a player's portrait ONLY with `Arcade.portraitHTML(memberId, {size, full, skin, label, cls, color})`, never an `<img>` of your own. It returns a square `.pt-box` (theme.css) with the SVG portrait underneath and the image on top, hidden until it loads; a missing or broken file tries the next candidate (`optimized/<name>.webp` if listed in `OPTIMIZED`, `<name>.png`, `<name>.webp`) and ends on the SVG, so a broken image never shows. `full: true` (the Select Player preview only) tries `<name>-full` first. `skin` ({color, acc}; left out = the instrument's equipped skin, `false` = none) draws the SKINS below and tries the drawn variants `<name>--<color>` / `<name>--<acc>` (and `-full--`) first; failed variant/-full URLs are remembered per tab in sessionStorage. `color` (recolored CPU rivals) or `'cpu'` = SVG only, no skin. File names are in `FILES` in portraits.js (member id → file name). Images are `object-fit: contain` on transparent backgrounds; markers (1P/2P) and outlines sit above them (`z-index`). An image over 250 KB or 1024 px gets a WebP copy in `shared/portraits/optimized/` (≤ 512 px; ≤ 1024 px tall for `-full`) listed in `OPTIMIZED`; the original is never touched, and replacing it means deleting its copy.
- SKINS (`shared/skins.js`, loaded right after portraits.js by EVERY page that shows a portrait; those pages also load `games.js`, which `allStars` needs for `byMember` games): one color skin + one accessory per instrument MEMBER, saved on the device (`store.skin(m)` / `setSkin(m, patch)`; shown ones in `store.skinsSeen(key)` / `markSkinsSeen`, key = member for star milestones, `'*'` for achievements). `Arcade.Skins`: `LIST` (the `SKINS` table: `{id (never change), kind 'color'|'acc', name, unlock, look | art}`), `ANCHORS` (accessory positions per instrument: `img`/`svg`/optional `full` × `head [x,y,w]`, `face [x,y,w,tilt]`, optional `back [x,y,w]`, all in percent of the square portrait; mirrored as a table in shared/portraits/README.md), `ACC_ART`, `isUnlocked(skin, member)`, `requirement(skin)`, `progress(skin, member)`, `milestone(skin)`, `equipped(member)` (a locked choice falls back to Classic Neon / none), `equip(member, {color}|{acc})`, `label(eq)` (alt text: "Trumpet, Flame skin, Crown"), `fresh(member)`, `announce(panel, {members, member})`, `catchUp(member)`, `refresh(member)`, `decorate()` (portraits.js's hook). UNLOCK RULES: `{always}`, `{stars: n}` = `store.allStars(member)` ≥ n (per instrument), `{game, level, stars, text}` = `store.bestLevelStars(game, level)` (any progress key of that game, any instrument) ≥ stars (`suffix: ':extra'` = only keys ending in it), `{game, badge: true}` = any Ancient Ninja Scrolls badge, `{game, achievement: id}` = `store.gameData(game).achievements[id]` (Sustain Speedway's `'virtuoso-win'`; Arcade Quest's `'ep1'` → Pixel Hero and `'manor-friends'` → Baton, set by `Q.save.achievements()`); achievements unlock for every instrument; a rule for a game missing from games.js is skipped. Rules are checked from saved progress (never only at the moment of a result), so old progress counts. EVERY RESULTS SCREEN calls `A.Skins.announce(panel)` after it shows (Chime Heist: `{members: ['bells'], member: 'bells'}`; Neon Face-Off: vs CPU only; the scrolls' Belt Exam badge panel too); Select Player calls `catchUp(store.player)` on load. Never show an UNLOCKED! card during play. Effects: CSS in theme.css "Skins" (`.sk-*`), drawn parts (flame aura, sparkles) in skins.js; tokens only; animation ONLY on `.pt-box-big` and never under reduced motion; image effects only once the picture loaded (`.pt-ok`), the SVG fallback just recolors (`svgColor`/`svgGlow`); a drawn variant sets `.pt-var-c` (effects off) / `.pt-var-a` (overlay off); Pixel redraws the loaded picture into a small canvas (`.pt-pix`). Portrait CSS must never style `.pt-box svg` descendants (the accessory is an `svg` too): use `.pt-box>svg.portrait`. `?demo&unlockall` previews everything (never saved, never announced).
- NOTE SEQUENCES (`shared/sequences.js`, on top of scales.js): THE way a note-reading game gets its notes. `Arcade.buildSequence({member, group, notes, order, level, count, pool})` -> `{items, pool, fit, sig, scale, name(pc), small}`; each item `{n (written note, key-correct spelling: answers use it), show (how to draw it), label, midi (written), sounding, pc}`. NOTES: `'first5'` (the group's first five; Horn's Starting notes pick the group) | `'Bb'|'Eb'|'F'|'Ab'` (the member's one-octave written scale from scales.js, 8 notes, same octave as scale mode) | `'chrom'` (the member's whole GMEA range). ORDER: `'random'` (bag shuffle: every pool note before any repeats, never the same pitch twice in a row; the level's `pool` below 5, i.e. level 1, uses the smaller pool: first three / the scale's first five / one octave from the first-five tonic; chromatic black keys spelled ♯ or ♭ at random) | `'order'` (up then down, then again from the second note; first5 = the five up and down, chrom = up in sharps, down in flats). Scale pools draw the key signature in both orders (`sig`, `show`); first5 and chrom draw accidentals, no key signature. Also `Arcade.progressKey(gameId, notes, order)`, `Arcade.progressKeys(gameId)` (all 12), `Arcade.NOTE_CHOICES`, `Arcade.ORDER_CHOICES`.
- MODE PICKER (`shared/mode-picker.js`, styles `.mp*` in theme.css): `Arcade.ModePicker.mount(el, {gameId, levels, onChange, group?, member?})` draws NOTES (six buttons, each with its stars for the chosen ORDER out of the game's `maxStars` from games.js) and ORDER (Random | Scale Order) plus a line explaining the choice; remembered per game (`store.noteMode`/`setNoteMode`; `memory: key` remembers it elsewhere, e.g. Neon Face-Off's `'neon-face-off:p1'`/`':p2'`; `stars: false` hides the star totals; `keySuffix: () => ':extra'` reads stars from the keys + that suffix, `max` overrides the per-combination maximum; `picker.refresh()` redraws). `picker.state` = `{notes, order, member, group, scale, progressKey, label}`. Helpers: `ModePicker.sequence(state, L, lv)` (buildSequence for a level row), `levelText(state, L, lv, extraParts)` (the game's own blurb for First 5 + Random, else what the level plays + the game's parts), `hubCard(state)`, `useRange(state)`, `demoSpace(fn)`. Used by Ghost Notes, Note Storm, Note Ninja, Chime Heist (group/member = the bells) and Neon Face-Off (one per player, no stars); NOT by Button Masher, Ancient Ninja Scrolls or the Note Checker.
- PROGRESS KEYS for note-reading games (never rename; `KEY_OVERRIDES` in sequences.js keeps a game's older keys):

| NOTES | ORDER | Progress key | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| First 5 | Random | `<game>` | the original key: every star from before scales still counts here |
| First 5 | Scale Order | `<game>:order-first5` | new |
| Concert B♭ / E♭ / F / A♭ | Scale Order | `<game>:scale-Bb` … `:scale-Ab` | the original SCALES keys |
| Chromatic | Scale Order | `<game>:scale-chrom` (Chime Heist: `chime-heist:chromatic`) | the original keys |
| Concert B♭ / E♭ / F / A♭ | Random | `<game>:random-Bb` … `:random-Ab` | new |
| Chromatic | Random | `<game>:random-chromatic` (Chime Heist: `chime-heist:full`, its old FULL RANGE) | new (Chime Heist: kept) |

- RHYTHMS AND COUNTING (Rhythm Dojo; any game that shows a rhythm uses these, never its own):
  - `shared/counting.js` = MR. GRAHAM'S COUNTING. `Arcade.Counting.parse(text, time)` reads rhythms as TEXT (`w h q e s`, `.` dotted, `r` rest, `_` tied to the next note, `[e e e]` a triplet, `|` bar lines, checked) into TICKS (a quarter = 12; 6/8's eighth-note beat = 6); `groups(parsed)` → one counting group per note (a tie chain is ONE group, across bar lines too) or rest: `{big (the starting syllable), small: [{text, t}] (every grid point held through, strictly inside), rest, grid}`; `text(groups)` → `'1^(& 2) & (3^(4))'` (plain text: `^( )` = small, `( )` = rest). Syllables: 1 2 3 4 · 1 & 2 & · 1 e & a · 1 la le (triplets); 3/4 and 2/4 the same with fewer beats; 6/8 = 1 2 3 4 5 6 (the eighth gets the beat), its sixteenths 1 & 2 & …. THE RULE: a note's small syllables use the COARSEST grid (quarter → eighth → triplet → sixteenth; 6/8: eighth → sixteenth) on which both its start and its end fall. `rhythm-dojo/examples.js` = the Counting Board's examples with the counting each must show (the tests check every one).
  - `shared/rhythm-staff.js` = THE RHYTHM STAFF: `Arcade.RhythmStaff.engrave(parsed, {from, to, counting, showTime, id, groups})` → `{svg, w, h, xAt(tick), xs, bars, t0, t1}`: a ONE-LINE PERCUSSION STAFF drawing exactly the written values (stems up, beams by beat: 6/8 by dotted quarter, rests break them, sixteenths' secondary beams and stubs; flags, dots, ties under the heads (half ties at a row's edges), triplet 3s / brackets, rests, time signature, bar lines, final bar) with the counting underneath: the starting syllable full size, the held ones SMALL + RAISED with ONE underline under them only, rests in parentheses. Spacing grows with the note's length (log) and widens wherever a note's counting needs room. `rows(parsed, perRow)` splits long rhythms into rows (a tie or a counting group continues on the next row); `refine(root)` re-measures the counting with the real font once the SVG is in the page. Classes for a game's CSS: `g.rn` (a note, data-g = its group), `g.rc` (a counting group), `.rc-big`, `.rc-small` (data-t = tick), `.rc-par`, `.rc-u`; colors are currentColor. (Music Highway's notation.js is different: it spells durations itself from a melody.)
  - `shared/onsets.js` = CLAPS AND HITS: sharp onsets from the energy of the pre-emphasized signal in 128-sample frames (`RULES`: a frame ≥ `rise` × the background of `bgFrom`–`bgTo` ms before, above the gate = `gateK` × the sensitivity slider's gate, `refractoryMs` 85), NEVER pitch. `Arcade.Onsets.analyse(samples, sr)` (recordings, tests), `listen(fn)` → `{stop()}` (fn `{time (performance ms, sample-accurate: counted from the stream), level}`), reading the same microphone as pitch.js (`Pitch.source()`) through a ScriptProcessor, only while `Pitch.listening()` and never during `Pitch.suppress`; `watchNode(node)` (tests: the game's own output looped back), `fake(time)` (?demo keys), `log`.
  - `shared/calibration.js` = THE AUDIO CLOCK + THE TIMING CHECK (moved out of Music Highway, which uses it too): `Arcade.AudioClock.create()` → `{start(), sample(), audAt(perfMs) (the AUDIBLE context time), now(), ctx, out, off}`; `Arcade.Calibration.analyse({clicks, lead, attacks, audAt, rules, accept})` → `{offs, median, spread, accepted, bleed, clickLike, ok, why}` (the median attack-to-click offset; a round that is machine-steady near 0 ms or far too early is the click itself: refused). THE SAVED DELAY (one per device, shared by Rhythm Dojo and Showtime Malfunction's snare): `Calibration.saved(key)` / `store(key, res)` = `gameData('rhythm-dojo').calib[key]` ('clap' | 'tap'; the snare uses 'clap'), `lag(key)` = the saved ms, else `RD_RULES.defaultLag`, else `DEFAULT_LAG`.
  - `shared/rhythm-judge.js` = THE RHYTHM JUDGE (moved out of Rhythm Dojo, same results): `Arcade.RhythmJudge.match(targets, attacks, {perfectMs, goodMs, okMs, lateMs})` → each attack claims the nearest unjudged note within `lateMs` (PERFECT / GOOD / OK / EARLY / LATE), leftover notes MISS, leftover attacks EXTRA. Rhythm Dojo and the snare's rhythm/accent machines use it; never a second judge.
  - `shared/timing-check.js` = THE TIMING CHECK FLOW (moved out of Rhythm Dojo): `Arcade.TimingCheck.open({els: {panel, title, msg, dots, say, pad, go, skip}, tap, first, rules, clock, kit, key, gameId, words, onClose(ok)})` (4 clicks to listen to, then 8 to clap/tap on; `Sfx.outputRaw` clicks; `Calibration.analyse`, then `store(key)`), `state()` = `{clicks, last, running}`. Each game passes its own panel's elements (Dojo `#cal*`, Showtime `#stCal*`); the words default to Dojo's.
- `Arcade.requireMic(fn)` shows the "Turn on the microphone" reminder ONCE PER PLAY SESSION, then runs `fn`: in later games the microphone starts straight from the tap (the browser's permission request still happens when needed, and the screen always shows with its fix-it steps when the mic is blocked or missing); `requireMic.showing()` for tests.
- THE PLAY SESSION (`Arcade.session`, shared/version.js: `has(k)`, `mark(k)`, `reset()`): once-per-session flags (the mic reminder `'mic-reminder'`, Music Highway's `'mh-calibrated-speaker'` / `'-headphones'`). A session = from opening the arcade until the tab is closed or a page is RELOADED: the flags live in memory and are handed to the next page of the same tab through `window.name` (never localStorage/sessionStorage); a reload (navigation type 'reload') starts a new one. Tests: tests/play-session.spec.js.
- Links: `Arcade.link(path)` keeps the query string. `Arcade.linkTo(path, {game})` keeps `?demo` but sets or drops `?game=` (use it for links between pages). `Arcade.playerLink(gameId, root)`, `Arcade.homeLink(gameId, root)`; `root` is `'../'` from a game folder (the default) or `''` from the site root.
- SOUND (`shared/sounds.js` = THE SOUND LIST, `shared/sfx.js` = the player; both on every page, sounds.js first; Mat's files in `shared/sounds/<file>.m4a|.mp3`, listed with names, screens, timing and lengths in `shared/sounds/README.md`, which must list every event). sounds.js: `Arcade.Sounds.LIST[event] = {file, vol, loop, mic, play, screen, when, len, gen?, fallback?}`, `Sounds.add({...})` (a game adds its own events without editing sfx.js; `gen` = its built-in fallback beeps), `Sounds.get(name)` (makes `select-<gameId>` for every game in games.js, falling back to `select-default`), `Sounds.names()`, `Sounds.SCREENS`. sfx.js: `Arcade.Sfx.event(name)` → seconds played (0 = muted / before the first tap / not allowed): the file (.m4a then .mp3, fetched + decoded, or an `<audio>` element on file://) → its `fallback` event's file → the built-in sound for that action (`EVENTS`, the old generated sounds; `CURRENT` maps new names to old sounds: wheel-* = whoosh, select-default = coin, player-continue = player-select, ui-toggle = blip, lobby-ambience = the hum) → a generic retro beep (`GENERIC`). Missing files: `console.info` only (once per tab). An EFFECT's 404 (or undecodable file) is remembered in sessionStorage for the tab for 10 minutes (`MISS_MS`, then asked again: iPad home-screen apps keep a "tab" for days); MUSIC (loop) files are never remembered as missing, and a music 404 is asked again with `cache: 'no-cache'` (a file uploaded after a browser cached its GitHub Pages 404 must still play: music has no built-in fallback). Network errors are never remembered; wanted music retries after one (3 tries). A file is missing only after BOTH .m4a and .mp3 fail. After the first unlock the wanted track loads first, then the other preloaded music one file at a time. The unlock listeners re-arm whenever the AudioContext stops running (iPad: another app, a refused resume), so the next tap resumes everything. `?debug` on any page = the SOUND LOG (console + a small box on the page: the track wanted, every URL tried for music AND effects, loaded/decode/404/skipped, start/stop, why it's waiting, and why an effect played its fallback: not downloaded yet / still downloading / missing; `Sfx.musicLog`). `Sfx.sequence([names], gap = 80, {channel, maxStep})` (each when the last ENDS: an effect's length is where its sound ends, trailing silence not counted (`audibleEnd`; the Sound Board flags > 0.5 s of silence); a VOICE line waits until any voice speaking has finished; `maxStep` ms caps a wait; returns `{cancel()}`), `Sfx.cancelAll(channel)` (drops pending sequences; a channel includes its `'<channel>:…'` sub-channels), `Sfx.pending(channel)`, `Sfx.hush()` (fades out, 0.15 s, any VOICE still speaking: a new scene), `Sfx.preloadScreen(screen)` (load + decode every effect of that sounds.js screen now: a game's setup screen). NOTHING PLAYS LATE: a sound not decoded yet plays its generated fallback at once, and while the AudioContext is stopped (iPad interruptions) sounds are SKIPPED (except `NOW_MS` 400 ms after a tap asked it to start), never scheduled to burst out on resume; on file:// an `<audio>` copy that can't start within 400 ms is dropped. sounds.js `voice: true` (the Sensei, the countdown voice, the announcer) = never over itself (`maxInstances` 1; any sound can set `maxInstances`), `busy('voice')`, `Sfx.use(...screens)` (preload this page's sounds after the first tap, 2 at a time; mountTopbar calls `use('game', gameId)`), `Sfx.playThenGo(name, href)` (changes page when the sound ends, ≤ 1.5 s: the floor's START plays `select-<gameId>` THERE, never on the game page; it waits for a still-downloading file like `eventSoon`), `Sfx.prefer(names)` (small effects loaded NOW, ahead of the queue; before the first tap they're downloaded on their own, a missing .m4a remembered, and decoded on unlock: the floor prefers the front cabinet's `select-<id>` and its two neighbors on every turn, because the `select-<id>` events come LAST in the normal preload order), `Sfx.duck(ms, {level = 0.4, down = 0.2, up = 0.5})` (voice lines: dips the MUSIC and AMBIENCE channels to `level` over `down` s, holds ms, back up over `up` s; its own gain node after each channel's slider, so mute/sliders never undo it; overlapping ducks keep the later end; `musicState()` reports `duck`), `Sfx.busy()` (ms until the last effect played ends), `Sfx.started` (a tap on this page has started the audio), `Sfx.eventSoon(name, ms = 600)` (like `event`, but waits up to ms for a file still downloading instead of playing the fallback: the floor's START), THE MUSIC MANAGER (`CH` in sfx.js: two channels, MUSIC on `musVol` and AMBIENCE on `ambVol`, each with a WANTED track and what's playing): `Arcade.sfx.setMusic(name | [names best first] | null, {builtIn})` / `setAmbience(...)` any time (before the first tap, before the file has loaded); `apply()` starts it with a fade-in as soon as the audio is unlocked AND the file is decoded (files wanted before the first tap are fetched early and decoded on unlock), never drops a request, never restarts the track already playing, crossfades (0.5 s) to a different one, falls back to the next name / the built-in version (`builtIn`: the room hum, the original chiptune `CHIP` rendered once with OfflineAudioContext) / silence, respects mute and the sliders, stops while listening (`Sfx.sync()` after listening starts or stops) and pauses with a hidden tab (the context is suspended). `Sfx.preloadMusic([names])` fetches likely next tracks; `Sfx.musicState()` for tests. Who wants what: the floor `setAmbience('lobby-ambience', {builtIn: true})` (+ preloads `select-music` and the front cabinet's `select-music-<id>`), the select view `setAmbience(null)` + `setMusic(['select-music-<id>', 'select-music'], {builtIn: true})` (null as the game opens), Arcade Quest file-only tracks per scene/room; Lost Signal `lost-signal-music` on its level screens only; every other game its GAME MENU MUSIC: `Sfx.gameMenuMusic(gameId, on = true, {afterEffects})` = games.js `menuMusic` (the 9 `<game>-menu` loops), else `select-music` (else the built-in chiptune), fading in 0.8 s (`MENU_XF`) on menu screens (hub, mode picker, intro panels, results after their sounds: `afterEffects`); a listening game's mic is PAUSED on menus (`Pitch.pauseListening`) so music can play; `false` at every level start: the music fades out 0.5 s (`MENU_FADE`) while the mic is back but hears nothing new (`Pitch.suppress`). Games with `menuMusicOwn` (Dojo Duel, Lost Signal, Vanishing Ink, Arcade Quest) run their own music and never call it. `playThenGo` fades music + ambience 0.8 s as the page changes (the wish stays for a Back-button restore); the select view's music fades 0.8 s as a game opens. The Sound Board's MUSIC section lists every menu track (✓ uploaded / missing — what plays instead) with Play/Stop. File loops get a baked crossfade so they wrap seamlessly (`select-music-<id>` counts as a loop file), `Sfx.mountControls(el, {lobby})` (the top bar's SETTINGS button, a speaker showing sound on/off: it opens THE SETTINGS PANEL of the UI kit; mountTopbar mounts it on every game; `Sfx.settingsChanged(k, done)`, `loopNote()`, `refreshControls()` are what the panel calls), `Sfx.outputRaw()` (the effects output WITHOUT the EFFECTS slider, SOUND ON/OFF still mutes: only Music Highway's calibration clicks), `Sfx.bell(soundingMidi)` (Chime Heist's bars: ALWAYS generated, never files), `Sfx.piano(soundingMidi)` (Keys to the City's touch-mode keys: a generated piano note with a hammer tap and a natural decay; returns false and plays nothing while a microphone listens), `Sfx.history` (last 60 plays: tests), `Sfx.board` (the Sound Board page, `sound-board/index.html`: not linked from the arcade; every section (Music, the Arcade Quest rooms, one per `Sounds.SCREENS` screen) is a `<details>` that starts COLLAPSED, its summary = the name, "· 24 sounds" and a "⚠ 2 missing, 1 too long" badge from the rows' statuses; EXPAND ALL / COLLAPSE ALL; a SEARCH box (name, what it's for, file, screen: matching sections open, the rest hide, "No sounds match", clearing restores what was open); the open sections are remembered for the browser session (sessionStorage `bandarcade.sb-open`); `sound-board/index.html#<sound name>` opens its section and scrolls to its row, highlighted; `Arcade.SoundBoard.state()` for tests (tests/sound-board.spec.js); it checks every file on open and with RELOAD ALL SOUNDS, always bypassing caches: `board.load(name)` = `fetch(url + '&t=<now>', {cache: 'reload'})`, one download per file per check). CACHING: every sound URL is `<file>.<ext>?v=<SOUNDS_VERSION>` (the `var` at the very top of sounds.js, `Sounds.VERSION`); games use the normal browser cache (never no-cache/reload there), and Mat bumps SOUNDS_VERSION by 1 after replacing or deleting sounds. The per-tab missing-file memory is keyed by the versioned URL, so a bump clears it too. The AudioContext starts on the first pointerdown/keydown (the one exception: `Sfx.autoStart(ms)`, PRESS START's check on load: it makes the context, which stays suspended until the first tap unless the browser already allows sound, and resolves true if it is running within ms). Settings: `store.sfx`/`setSfx(on)` (default on), `store.sfxVol` / `store.musVol` / `store.ambVol` / `setVolume(k, 0–1)` (defaults 0.6, 0.4 and 0.3; `store.ambience` = ambVol > 0). Events wired today: floor `wheel-left`, `wheel-right`, `cabinet-focus`, `select-<id>`, `zone-select` (a zone sign tapped), `zone-enter` (its cabinets appear), `zone-back` (BACK / ← LOBBY), `all-games-open`, the Prize Counter's `prize-hello` (optional voice), `prize-tokens`, `prize-win`; Select Player `select-music` (loop), `choose-instrument` (the announcer, as the view opens), `tile-move`, `player-select`, `player-continue`, `player-ready`, `player2-join`, `skin-equip`, `skin-unlocked`; `ui-back` (← ARCADE), `ui-toggle` (sound, horn, NOTES × ORDER); Create Your Player `avatar-open` (the editor opens), `avatar-change` (any option), `avatar-randomize` (SURPRISE ME, SHUFFLE, random name), `avatar-save` (DONE); `item-unlocked` (an UNLOCKED! card with player items; falls back to skin-unlocked), Arcade Quest `item-purchase` (Token Booth: a player item or a charm; falls back to quest-tokens) and `charm-equip` (CHARMS panel, a charm reward; falls back to skin-equip); every game's `level-start`, `note-hit`, `note-wrong`, `note-missed`, `level-complete`, `level-failed`, `star-earned`, `new-high-score`; Note Storm `life-lost`, `game-over`; Endless mode `endless-start`, `speed-up`, `endless-life-lost`, `endless-game-over`, `endless-high-score`; Note Checker `all-notes-found`; plus each game's own (sounds.js). A new sound event goes in sounds.js AND shared/sounds/README.md.
- MENU BACKGROUNDS (`shared/bg-scenes.js` + `shared/backgrounds.js`, loaded right after sfx.js by every game page; `.bg-*` styles in theme.css): one fixed full-screen layer (`.bg-layer`, z-index −1, appended to body; `html.bg-on` makes the body's own background transparent while it shows) = Mat's picture (`shared/backgrounds/<id>.webp|.jpg|.png`, `<id>-portrait.*` on tall screens; misses remembered per tab in sessionStorage `bandarcade.bg-miss`; README there) ELSE the coded scene (games.js `bg: {scene, dim, focus}`) + the readability overlay (`.bg-dim`: `dim` everywhere, + `focus` in the middle). `Arcade.Bg.mount(gameId)` (mountTopbar does it), `Bg.menu(on)`: every menu screen on, play off (0.4 s crossfade; the scene keeps moving while it fades); `Sfx.gameMenuMusic` calls it, so the 9 games with menu music need nothing else; Lost Signal and Vanishing Ink call it from their `menuMusic(on)`, Dojo Duel on setup (on) / match (off), Arcade Quest in `Q.go` (the title only). Scenes: `Arcade.BgScenes.<scene>(ctx, W, H, t, {state, still, colors})` (storm, manor, bamboo, ink, vault, temple, arena, rink, stage, track, space, night-dojo, pixel-night, aurora; and, for avatar backgrounds so far, city, synthwave, galaxy, records, bubbles, lavalamp, confetti, fireflies; draw what never moves ONCE into `state` (pixel-night's dithered sky); `STILL[scene]` = the still moment; `flashes(scene, from, to)` for tests): DARK, low contrast, activity toward the edges, tokens only (`col()`/`mix()`). PHOTOSENSITIVITY: storm lightning = a glow BEHIND the clouds (`STRIKE`: every 6–15 s, 50 ms rise, 0.35–0.76 s fade incl. a 35 % chance of a weaker 2nd pulse 0.21 s later; measured ≤ 2 pulses a second, the brightest 1/96 of the screen +0.15 relative luminance, ≤ 1 % of the screen over the 0.1 flash threshold); stage `SPUTTER` = 3 smooth dips over 1.4 s every 8–13 s. PERFORMANCE: one scene, ≤ `FPS` 30, drawn at `RES` 0.6 of the screen (≤ `MAX_W` 1100 px) and scaled up, nothing while hidden or in play; frames averaging > `SLOW_MS` 40 over `SLOW_WINDOW` 3 s = still frames for good on this device (`gameData('bg').slow`); reduced motion or the MOTION switch (the Settings panel, shared/ui-kit.js: `gameData('bg').motion`) = the still frame. `Bg.state()` for tests. The ART BOARD (`art-board/index.html`, not linked): every game's scene (moving while on screen, with the overlay and a mock menu, "coded scene (default)" / "your image (overrides)"), its background / portrait / marquee slots, and every instrument portrait slot. A new game: add `bg: {scene, dim, focus}` in games.js.
- PRESS START (`shared/press-start.js`, `Arcade.PressStart.show(gameId, onStart)`; `.ps-*` in theme.css; loaded with `cabinets.js` + `marquees.js` after backgrounds.js by the 12 games): mountTopbar shows it ONCE per page load (never between a game's own screens): the game's marquee big (`Marquee.animate(el, 'title')`), over its menu background (`html.ps-on` hides the page except `#topbar`, which stays usable above the screen at `--ps-top`), "PRESS START" blinking slowly (1.1 s, soft; steady with reduced motion), "Tap anywhere or press any key", the game's stars (or its `summary`). The tap (pointerdown on the screen, or any key except Tab/Shift/Ctrl/Alt/Meta/Esc and keys on a top-bar button) runs after sfx.js's unlock in the same gesture: `Sfx.preloadScreen('game', 'general', <id>, 'endless')` (primes every effect), `eventSoon('press-start-<id>')` (falls back to `press-start`), the menu music (already wished for by the first screen; else `gameMenuMusic`), then a 0.4 s fade into the first screen already drawn underneath (`html.ps-reveal`). SKIPPED: `?nostart` (`?demo` still shows it), `Sfx.autoStart(300)` true (the browser already allows sound), a Back-button restore with the sound running. It never touches the microphone (requireMic at level start, as before). Arcade Quest (`pressStart: false`) keeps its own title; the Note Checker (a tool) has none. `PressStart.state()` for tests.
- THE LEVEL SELECT PATTERN (`shared/level-select.js`, loaded after press-start.js by every game; `.ls-*` in theme.css): SELECT, THEN START, like the note-set picker. `Arcade.LevelSelect.show({screen, grid, cards, unlocked(i), picker, endless, lockText(i), label(i), gameId, fresh, beside})` (`beside` = an element placed immediately LEFT of START in the sticky heading row, as tall as START, `.ls-beside`; on phones START still takes the rest of the line and never wraps; Tab reaches it before START; Sustain Speedway's GARAGE), called every time a game draws its level cards (i 0-based). HEADINGS: "① SELECT YOUR NOTES" (put at the top of the `picker`) and "② SELECT YOUR LEVEL" (no number without a picker) share ONE class, `.ls-head` (the announcer's neon lettering at section size, one line). The level heading sits in `.ls-row` with the START area (sticky at the top while the cards scroll under it). A tap/Enter/Space on a card SELECTS it (`.ls-sel`, `aria-pressed`: the chosen note set's look + a ✓; never starts the game: a capture-phase click listener on the grid stops the game's own handler); a locked card (`.ls-locked`, `aria-disabled`, the game's `disabled` removed so it can say so) shows `lockText` ("Clear Level N to unlock"; games say Vault/Belt/Showtime/Rival/Track) as a toast; the Endless tile (`endless` = its element) is selectable too ('endless'). Once a level is selected: START (`.ls-start`, "LEVEL 4 · FIRST FIVE": `label(i)` or the card's `.n` text + the picker's note set, "· SCALE ORDER" when it is) slides in with `start-ready`; before that "Select your notes and level" (`.ls-need`). START (tap/Enter/Space) re-clicks that card (or the Endless tile's `.ed-go`) with the listener standing aside, so it runs the game's own handler exactly as a tap used to (requireMic, story panels, intro screens). A new note set keeps the level and updates the line. REMEMBERED: `gameData('level-select')['<game>|<player>|<instId>']` = the index or 'endless', saved on selection and by `Arcade.LevelSelect.played(i)`, which every game's start function calls (START, NEXT LEVEL, RETRY), so the screen comes back with the level just played; restored when the screen appears (nothing if it's locked now or gone); first visit = nothing selected. THE SCREEN APPEARING (the first call, or the first after the screen was hidden: a MutationObserver) waits for PRESS START's tap, then: the `select-level` voice line ("Select your level"; `Sfx.announce`: at most once a minute, sessionStorage `bandarcade.select-level-at`, only after a tap started the audio, held while an overlay is open, music ducked), the focus on START when it's ready, and after `IDLE_MS` 5 s with no tap or key the IDLE HINT (`.ls-hint`, a bouncing arrow) at what's missing: the level cards when none is selected, else START (never a particular level); any tap/key hides it, and it comes back only for the next missing step. No recommended level, no glow on any card. `LevelSelect.highlight({screen, el})` = a setup screen's main button (Dojo Duel's BEGIN THE DUEL, Neon Face-Off's START MATCH, Arcade Quest's CONTINUE/NEW GAME): lit (`.ls-main`), focus, idle hint; no heading or voice. LAYOUT: picker, then the half-size note staff (`.ls-notes` wraps `#modePick` + `.fivecard.ls-mini`; ≥ 700 px wide the staff sits beside the picker's ORDER row and line, where it was before, so it adds no height), then the heading row + the cards, extras below, so both headings, START and the first row of cards fit without scrolling on an iPad and at 1366 × 768. `LevelSelect.state()` = `{sel, ready, hint, hintAt, appeared, summary}` for tests.
- THE BAND NINJA CONNECTION (optional; `shared/bandninja.js`, loaded right after ui.js by every page; README "Band Ninja connection"): the arcade stays anonymous (sends nothing, stores nothing off the device). LINK SETTINGS `?inst=` (`INSTRUMENT_ALIASES` → `store.setPlayer`; unknown = ignored; the floor's `?game=` Select Player is skipped via `BandNinja.skipSelect()` in arcade.js), `?belt=` (Note Ninja) / `?rank=` (Ancient Ninja Scrolls): `BandNinja.levelWish(gameId)`, read ONCE by level-select.js at the screen's first appearance (the belt if open, else the highest open one + `.ls-bn` line); all three are applied once and removed from the address AND `A.params`. BELT REWARD CODES: `beltUnlockCode(belt)` is the portal's exact Apps Script algorithm (`ARCADE_CODE_SALT` must match the portal's; `selfTest()` in ?demo checks `EXPECTED`); `redeem(text)` (10 tries a minute, sessionStorage) saves `gameData('bandninja').belts` (in the Backup Code); Arcade Quest's Token Booth "Enter a code" = `Q.talk.beltCode()`, then `Skins.catchUp(member, {only: keys})`. OFFICIAL BAND NINJA GEAR = parts with `official: true` + `unlock: {bandninja: belt}` (avatar-parts.js end: the `belt` field `P.BN_BELTS`, drawn by avatar.js `beltLayer` with Z/z/Y; plates `bn-*`; top `bngi`; effect `bndiamond`; bg `bndojo` = bg-scenes `diamond-dojo`; colors `--bn-*` in theme.css): only a code opens it (never UNLOCK rules, stars or the shop); Create Your Player shows it only on its BAND NINJA tab, which appears once a code opened something (or ?unlockall); the Locker shows it only once earned. THE AVATAR CODE (`shared/avatar-code.js`, after avatar.js on every avatar page): `Arcade.avatarCode.encode/decode/valid/forDevice/share/load`; "BA1-" + base64url, a 12-bit check, then each TABLE field as an index (0 = default); TABLE is the format: APPEND only (new ids at the end of their list, new fields at the end); a new part must be added there (?demo warns). SHARE TO BAND NINJA in the creator + the badge menu; LOAD AVATAR CODE in the creator (locked items fall back, with a note). THE AVATAR CARD (`avatar-card/index.html?code&frame&still&name`): only avatar-parts/names, avatar.js, avatar-code, bg-scenes, avatar-bg, avatar-fx (no storage.js: no reads or writes, no unlock checks), `Arcade.avatarStill` (still=1) stops the animation loop, `color-scheme: normal` so it's see-through in any iframe; never add frame-busting or anything that blocks embedding.
- THE AVATAR BADGE (`shared/avatar-badge.js`, loaded right after avatar.js by every page with a top bar; `.avb*` in theme.css): `Arcade.AvatarBadge.mount(el, {member, instLabel, changeInstrument: href | fn | null, onEdit})` = a ≥ 44 px pill with the avatar's bust, its safe-builder name, the instrument and a pencil (no avatar saved yet: a silhouette + "Create avatar"); tapping it opens a small menu: EDIT AVATAR (the creator as an OVERLAY on this page: the screen, the zone/cabinet, the note set, the selected level and the scroll stay exactly as they were) and CHANGE INSTRUMENT (omitted on Choose Your Instrument itself). Tap outside or Esc closes it (focus back on the badge), ↑/↓ move. mountTopbar puts it in every game's top bar (instead of the old instrument chip); the floor's `.fbar` (`#avBadge`) and the select view's top bar (`#spBadge`) mount their own. `AvatarBadge.edit({member, onClose})` opens the creator for the device's avatar, loading avatar.css + shared/instrument-sprites.js + avatar-creator.js first when the page doesn't have them (through `Arcade.v`). DURING PLAY it is hidden: `Bg.menu(false)` (every level/round/match/battle/transmission start) sets `html.in-play`, `Bg.menu(true)` (menus, results) clears it. Neon Face-Off's Player 1 picture on its setup opens the editor too (Dojo Duel's already did).
- HOLD GUARD (`Arcade.holdGuard(el, {lock, touch})`, ui.js; `.hold-guard` / `.hold-lock` in theme.css): every on-screen control that is HELD or tapped fast (Arcade Quest's pad and game area, Button Masher's fingering diagram, Note Ninja's answer pad; Chime Heist's kit, Dojo Duel and AnswerPad already had the same CSS) never selects text, shows the iPad callout/magnifier, flashes a tap highlight, or (`lock`) scrolls/zooms: no selection/callout/highlight CSS, contextmenu/selectstart/dragstart blocked, `lock` = `touch-action: none`, `touch` = a non-passive touchstart preventDefault (only where every control uses pointer events). Put no selectable text in such buttons (SVG icons, CSS content, aria-labels). Use it for any new held control.
- MUSIC THAT RESUMES: a loop with `resume: true` in sounds.js remembers where it was when it stopped (this page's session: `Sfx.trackPos()`), and starts there next time (Arcade Quest's room music after a battle or the microphone). `setMusic(names, {fade})`: the crossfade for this change (s).
- `Arcade.Sfx.announce(name, {key, gap, delay, alive(), hold(), onPlay})` (sfx.js): THE ANNOUNCER used by choose-instrument and select-level (see above); returns {cancel()}.
- THE COUNTDOWN (`shared/countdown.js`, loaded after sfx.js by Dojo Duel, Neon Face-Off and Sustain Speedway): `Arcade.countdown({style, voicePrefix, later, show, onGo, steps, go, delay})` = the beats and their sounds only (each game draws its own numbers in `show(label, kind)`, kind 'count'|'ready'|'go', and runs the timers on its own clock with `later(ms, fn)`). Styles: CLASSIC (3-2-1, `steps.classic` 1000 ms, spoken `<prefix>-count-3/-2/-1`), QUICK (0.35 s steps, the `<prefix>-count` tick, else `dojo-count`), READY-GO ("READY…" + `<prefix>-ready`, then "GO!", `steps.ready` 450 ms each), OFF (a `steps.off` pause). `go: true` = a spoken "GO!" beat (`<prefix>-count-go`) whose voice ENDS before `onGo` (at most `steps.goMax`), so it never overlaps the moment a player must play; Dojo Duel uses `go: false` (its note's own "Go!"). Voice prefixes 'dojo', 'faceoff' and 'race' (every faceoff-* / race-* sound falls back to the matching dojo-count-* file, then the dojo-count tick; a new prefix needs only its sounds.js entries). `countdown.sounds(prefix)` = every sound a CLASSIC + GO countdown can play, fallbacks included (for `Sfx.prefer` / `Sfx.whenReady`). Beats never exceed 3 a second; games only scale numbers in (no flash), steady under reduced motion.
- sounds.js `cap` (dB, the LOUDNESS CAP; Showtime Malfunction's scare stings): sfx.js scales the file so its peak and its loudest 50 ms (RMS) stay within cap dB of the arcade's normal loudest effect (the loudest uncapped file played on the page, at least `REF_PEAK` .9 / `REF_RMS` .38, measured from shared/sounds), then a hard limiter (WaveShaper) at the peak limit; the generated fallback goes through the same limiter; `Sfx.loudness()` for tests. TEACHER SETTINGS: `shared/teacher-settings.js` (`Arcade.TEACHER`: switches Mat edits, never saved on a device; today `JUMP_SCARE_ALLOWED`).
- `Sfx.event(name, {muteCap})`: this play mutes the microphone at most `muteCap` ms, echo included (Neon Face-Off's in-rally sounds); the sound itself plays on. sounds.js `maxLen` (s): the Sound Board warns when the uploaded file is longer ("Trim this sound to under 0.3 s for the snappiest rallies").
- `Arcade.requireInstrument(gameId)`: the saved instrument, or redirect to Select Player (returns null).
- Cabinets (`shared/cabinets.js` + `shared/cabinets.css`): `Arcade.cabinetHTML(game, {href})`, `Arcade.marqueeHTML(game)`, `Arcade.trimClasses(game)`, `Arcade.cabinetOf(game)` (settings with defaults), `Arcade.setAttract(cabEl, game)` (only the front cabinet animates). A CANVAS SCREEN (a `SCREENS` entry with `draw(ctx, W, H, t)` + an `html()` holding `.scr-cv canvas`, e.g. Music Highway) is drawn by the same function in 2D and 3D (`SCREENS3D[name]` calls it at 256 px): `Arcade.hydrateScreens(root)` (arcade.js, after the slots are built) draws each one's STILL frame (`t` = null), `setAttract` runs the front one's rAF loop (≤ `SCREEN_FPS` 30, dpr ≤ 1.5, nothing while hidden or `A.floorPaused`, none under reduced motion) and puts the old front back on its still frame.
- `Arcade.staffSVG(clef, items, {fit, label, width, captions, keySig, extra})` (items may set `whole`, `stemUp`, `stemTo`: Scale Trainer's rhythm picture; `extra` = SVG drawn last) (key signatures in the standard positions, any count, treble or bass; start notes `Arcade.keySigWidth(sig)` further right; `n.natural` draws ♮), `Arcade.noteGlyph(clef, {n, x, caption}, capY)` (one note, for games that move notes on their own layer), `Arcade.noteY(clef, n)`, `Arcade.fiveNoteStaff(inst, colorFor)`, `Arcade.colorNote(id, color)`, `Arcade.ghostSVG(text, cls)`, `Arcade.ninjaSVG({belt, cls, label})` (the Note Ninja mascot; colors resolved from tokens so it also works as a canvas image), `Arcade.starStr(n)`, `Arcade.mountTopbar(inst, extraHTML, gameId, {fixed})`, `Arcade.symbolSVG(id, label)` / `Arcade.symbolName(id)` / `Arcade.SYMBOL_IDS` (music symbols on a short staff: treble-clef, bass-clef, fermata, repeat, measure-repeat, accent).
- Belts (`shared/belts.js`): `Arcade.BELTS` = the 10 Band Ninja belts `{rank, name, color, sparkle}`, `Arcade.belt(rankOrName)`. The only place belt names/colors live; Note Ninja's levels.js matches by `name`, Ancient Ninja Scrolls uses ranks 3–10.
- BAND NINJA SHARED PIECES (ui.js + answer-pad.js): `Arcade.senseiSVG(mood 'calm'|'happy'|'hmm'|'present'|'ready'|'strike', belt)` = THE Sensei (Ancient Ninja Scrolls, Dojo Duel, Note Ninja: 'ready' = a wooden practice sword (bokken, `--sensei-bokken`) held calmly, 'strike' = mid-swing toward your left; `.ss-*` styles in theme.css); `Arcade.staffGuides(clef, x, alpha)` = the faint letter names after the clef (Note Ninja's belts, Dojo Duel); `Arcade.AnswerPad.mount(el, {accs, relabel, cls, onAnswer(letter, acc, ev)})` (shared/answer-pad.js, `.apad*` in theme.css) = Note Ninja's answer layout (♭ ♮ ♯ as a one-shot Shift, then A–G) as a component: answers on `pointerdown` (every finger its own pointer, so two pads work at once), `setAcc/press/set/lock/mark`. Note Ninja still has its own pad markup (same layout); new games use AnswerPad.
- `Arcade.store`: `exportAll()`/`importAll(obj)` (the whole device, for shared/backup.js), `allStars('*')` (every star on the device), `ownedItems`/`ownItem(key)` + `itemsSeen`/`markItemsSeen(keys)` (avatar items), `avatar`/`setAvatar(av)`, `guestAvatar`/`setGuestAvatar(av)`, `avatarOffered`/`setAvatarOffered()` (Create Your Player), `player`/`setPlayer(memberId)`, `instId` (the player's group), `hornStart`/`setHornStart('F'|'C')`, `pending`, `opponent`/`setOpponent(memberId | 'cpu')` + `opponentHornStart`/`setOpponentHornStart` (Player 2 in two-player games), **`allStars(instrument, gameId?)`** (THE star total: every progress key (all NOTES × ORDER combinations, every game's modes) for an instrument (a member id, or a player id like `'bells'`), for one game or all games; use it wherever a total is shown or checked: the floor's hi-score, the player card, future unlocks; `starsForPlayer(m)` = `allStars(m)`), `noteMode(gameId)`/`setNoteMode(gameId, {notes, order})` (reads choices saved by the old picker too), `sens`, `sfx`, `ambience`, `checkerMode` ('five'|'Bb'|'Eb'|'F'|'Ab'|'full'|'art'), `memberFor(groupId)`/`setMember(groupId, id)`, `level(game, inst, lvl)`, `setLevel(game, inst, lvl, {stars, best})`, `totalStars(game, inst)`, `hasProgress(game, inst)`, `gameData(gameId)` + `saveGameData(gameId)` (a game's own extra records, kept out of `games`), one-time moves in `migrate()` flagged in `data.migrated`. Progress shape: `games[gameKey][instId][level] = {stars, best}`. Note-reading games use the PROGRESS KEYS table above. The home page's hi-score shows `allStars` for the game ("all modes") and "Modes played: n of 12" (games.js `noteModes: true`).
- ENDLESS MODE (`shared/endless.js`, loaded after sfx.js by Note Storm and Note Ninja): `Arcade.Endless` = `speed(C, t)` (C.start + (C.max − C.start)(1 − e^(−t/C.k)) + C.creep·t), `mult(combo)` (×2 at 10, ×3 at 25, ×4 at 50: `COMBO`), `instKey(inst, member)` (the member; Horn adds its group), `setKey(pickerState)` (`'<notes>-<order>'`), `top()`, `tile(el, {gameId, instKey, setKey, label, blurb, onPlay})` (the ∞ card under the levels, always open, with the Top 5), `gameOver({…, run: {score, notes, speed, combo}, onAgain, onBack, backLabel})` (the GAME OVER panel: saves, Top 5 with this run highlighted, `endless-game-over` + `endless-high-score` for a new #1; calls `Skins.announce`), `hearts()`, `flash(el, text)`, a game may pass `title` to `tile` and `title`, `kicker`, `run.stats` ([[label, value]…] instead of the four standard boxes) and `sounds` ({over, top}) to `gameOver` (Lost Signal's Deep Space Scan) ("SPEED UP!": one gentle fade, never a strobe), `playerName()` (the avatar's name, else "Player"). Scores live in `store.endless` (`endlessTop(game, instKey, setKey)` / `addEndless(…)` keeps the best 5 `{score, name, date, notes, speed, combo}`), NEVER in `games`: no stars, no maxStars, no unlocks; the Arcade Backup Code carries them (it saves the whole store; the Quest code does not). Each entry also keeps the player's name `plate`, drawn in the Top 5. `?demo` runs never save ("Demo run — score not saved"); `Endless.saveInDemo = true` only while testing. Each game's numbers are one commented block in its levels.js (`STORM_ENDLESS`, `NINJA_ENDLESS`). Endless never pauses for a sound. Sounds (screen `endless`): `endless-start`, `speed-up` (during play), `endless-life-lost` (during play), `endless-game-over`, `endless-high-score`, each falling back to the older sound.
- PATTERNS (`shared/patterns.js`, Lost Signal + Vanishing Ink; loaded after mode-picker.js): THE pattern generator: short melodies from the NOTES × ORDER note set, moving by steps, small skips, larger skips. `Arcade.patterns.noteSet(pickerState, pool)` → `{set (one item per SOUNDING pitch, low to high, items as buildSequence makes them), sig, fit, name}` (pool 3 = the smaller level-1 pool, 5 = all); `generate(set, len, {leap, repeats}, gen)` (leap = biggest skip in half steps: 2 steps, 4 thirds, 7 fifths, 12 octave; repeats = twice in a row allowed, never three; rules with skips always have one in 3+ notes); `next(set, pattern, rules, gen)` (one more note: the endless modes grow a pattern); `GEN` = the default weights (step, third, fourth, fifth, wide, octave, repeat, recover, noBigInARow, noPingPong, mustSkip); a game passes its own (`SIGNAL_GEN`, `INK_GEN` in its levels.js; missing fields use GEN). Never write a pattern loop in a game.
- ECHO (`shared/echo.js`, Lost Signal + Vanishing Ink; after patterns.js): THE echo flow. `Arcade.Echo.create({slots, slotHTML, timer, slotMs, running(), answering(), pauseSuppressed, sounds: {ok, bad}, onFill(i, r), onDone(res), onMark(item)})` → `echo` with `begin(pattern)` (draws one `.slot` per note), `start()` (answering starts, or restarts the current slot's clock), `fill(pc)` (the note heard fills the next slot right or wrong, `null` = missed; plays the tiny ok/bad sound), `mark()`, `pulse(i)`, `want()`, `i`, `res` ([{pc, ok}]); its own 100 ms slot clock (paused while the tab is hidden, and while `Pitch.isSuppressed` with `pauseSuppressed`; Lost Signal leaves that off because its mic is paused for its sounds). `Echo.input({enabled(), onNote(pc, time)})` = the note input: each `Pitch.onAttack` with a pitch, and an `onHeld` note no attack was heard for within 700 ms (soft entries: a long note is ONE note, a repeat needs a new attack); `.reset()` per pattern. `Echo.drawResult(el, {clef, pattern, res, sig, fit, name, gap, minW, label, idPrefix, plain})` = the pattern on a staff in written pitch, centered after the clef + key signature: right gold, wrong coral + "you played D", missed coral in a dotted box; `plain: true` = the same layout with no names or marks (Vanishing Ink's scroll while studying, so the ink comes back in the same place). Styles (`.slot.cur/.ok/.bad/.miss/.rx`) are each game's.
- TONES (`shared/tones.js`, LOST SIGNAL ONLY, see Hard rules): `Arcade.tones.play(soundingMidis, {noteMs, gapMs, vol, onNote(i, midi)})` → `{dur, done (Promise), stop(), muted}` (a soft triangle + a touch of square, A440 equal temperament, via `Sfx.output()` = `{ctx, out}` once unlocked and sound is on, else `muted`), `tones.test()`, `tones.freq(midi)`, `tones.history` (tests). sounds.js `gen` entries may use type `'noise'` (`[freq | [from, to], at, len, vol, 'noise', q]`: band-passed noise, unpitched).
- `Arcade.DEMO` is true with `?demo`. Keys 1–5 fake the five notes, and games should unlock all levels.

## Adding a game
Make a new folder with `index.html` (the same `<head>` version lines and shared script tags as `ghost-notes/index.html`, including `games.js`, `scales.js`, `sequences.js` and `mode-picker.js`), `style.css` and `game.js`. Start `game.js` with `requireInstrument` + `mountTopbar(inst, '', GAME_ID)`. Load `portraits.js` + `skins.js` + `avatar-parts.js` + `avatar-names.js` + `avatar.js` + `games.js` + `sounds.js` + `sfx.js`, and call `A.Skins.announce($('results').querySelector('.panel'))` whenever the results screen shows (it also puts the player's avatar and name at the top). Show the PLAYER with `Arcade.avatarHTML({size, member})`, an INSTRUMENT with `portraitHTML`. A note-reading game gets its notes ONLY from `sequences.js` and its mode UI ONLY from `mode-picker.js`: mount `Arcade.ModePicker` on the level screen, build each level with `ModePicker.sequence(picker.state, L, lv)`, draw items' `show` with the result's `sig` and `fit`, check answers against `n` (or `pc`/`sounding` for the mic), save with `picker.state.progressKey`, and set `noteModes: true` in games.js. Never write a shuffle or scale loop in a game. First 5 + Random must behave exactly like the game always has (levels.js `pool: 3` on level 1). MENU MUSIC: give it `menuMusic: '<id>-menu'` in games.js + a loop entry in sounds.js (screen = its id) and call `A.Sfx.gameMenuMusic(GAME_ID)` on every menu screen, `(GAME_ID, false)` as a level starts, `(GAME_ID, true, {afterEffects: true})` when results show. Add it to `shared/games.js` (`maxStars` = levels × 3, or 0; `unpitched: true` only if it works for the Snare Drum; its `zones` and, if it doesn't suit every instrument, a `fit`: that's all the lobby needs) with a main `color` (`'pink'|'cyan'|'yellow'`), a `cabinet` entry and a `marquee` entry (its lit sign's scene: see MARQUEES). Reuse `theme.css` components (`.stage`, `.hear`, `.overlay/.panel`, `.btn-*`). Its first screen uses the LEVEL SELECT PATTERN: load `level-select.js`, call `A.LevelSelect.show(...)` wherever the level cards are drawn (picker + the `.ls-notes` staff first, then the cards, extras below) and `A.LevelSelect.played(lv - 1)` at the top of the level's start function; keep the cards' own click handlers (START runs them).
- Cabinets: each game's look is its `cabinet` field in `games.js` (`shape`, `trim`, `trim2`, `marquee`, `screen`; all optional, missing or unknown values fall back to a classic cabinet). `cabinet.marquee` now picks only the title's lettering (and the 2D sign's frame shape); the picture behind it is the game's own `marquee` entry (MARQUEES below). The parts it picks from live in `shared/cabinets.js` (`SHAPES` silhouettes on a 300 × 600 grid, `SCREENS` attract loops) and `shared/cabinets.css` (trim colors, marquee lettering, screen styles). Give a new game its own silhouette/screen rather than reusing another game's whole look. Never let a missing cabinet entry break the home page.
- MARQUEES (`shared/marquees.js`, loaded by index.html after cabinets.js): every cabinet's lit sign is a canvas drawing: a themed animated SCENE + the title. One renderer for the 3D texture (arcade3d.js `shared.marquee`: 512 px wide, redrawn only for the FRONT cabinet at `Marquee.FPS` = 20, half that once the 3D floor has downgraded; the old front goes still on a turn), the 2D cabinets (`Arcade.marqueeHTML` = `Marquee.html`: a `.mq-live` element with a canvas + the name for screen readers; `Marquee.hydrate(root)` draws still frames; `setAttract` calls `Marquee.animate(el, 'floor')`) and Select Player (`animate(el, 'select')`, stopped by `close()`). Each game's look = its `marquee` entry in games.js `{scene, colors (theme tokens, in the scene's order), speed, still, every, titleLayouts}`. **Cabinet marquees show only the game's main title, fit as large as possible (titleFit:'max'). No subtitles, taglines or small text on marquees.** ('max' is the only mode, so a new game gets it automatically; there is no `kicker` any more; 2-player and other info goes on the lobby's cards, e.g. ALL GAMES' 2P / ASSIGNED badges beside the name, never over the picture.) `fitMax` tries 1 line or 2 stacked lines (every split at a space; never inside a word or at a hyphen: NEON / FACE-OFF; or the game's `titleLayouts`) and keeps the biggest; it measures each line's real glyph bounds (first/last letter overhangs, letter-by-letter advances, a space at least `WORD_GAP` 0.28 em because GN Neon's space is a hairline) + outline (`STROKE`) + visible glow (`GLOW × GLOW_REACH`) + slant, keeps `TITLE_MARGIN` × H + the border + 2 px on every side, all lines one size, `LINE_GAP` between them. FONTS FIRST: `fontReady(font)` = the title is measured and drawn only once its lettering font has loaded (`document.fonts.check`/`load`; until then the sign shows its scene alone; a font that never arrives falls back after `FONT_WAIT` 3 s), and `redrawAll()` redraws every 2D sign, 3D texture (`onArt`) and thumbnail the moment it arrives (and on `loadingdone`); cached per (game, W, H, font loaded), so it re-fits on resize too; `fitInfo(g, W, H)` (`{size, lines, font}`) and `draw(…, {titleOnly: true})` for tests; scenes in `SCENES` (storm, manor, vu, dojo, vault, scroll, versus, hockey, curtain, synthwave, pixel, radio, duel, ink, sparkle = the default: the game's color, moving gradient, sparkles). The title's font comes from `cabinet.marquee` (`FONTS`); it always gets a dark outline + dark haze so it stays readable. Only the front (and Select Player) animates; `prefers-reduced-motion` = still frames everywhere; nothing while the tab is hidden or `A.floorPaused`. PHOTOSENSITIVITY RULE: nothing may flash more than 3 times a second; lightning, flickers and sparks light only PART of the sign and fade in/out (storm `every` ≥ 1.2 s; flickers < 1 Hz, smooth). Test with the luminance-sampling check (WCAG 0.1 threshold) before adding a scene. MAT'S ART: `shared/marquees/<id>.png|.webp` replaces the scene (title still drawn), `<id>-full.png|.webp` the whole sign (1200 × 300 recommended; shared/marquees/README.md); misses remembered per tab (sessionStorage `bandarcade.mq-miss`); the 3D view skips pictures on file:// (a tainted canvas can't be a WebGL texture). `Marquee.onArt(g, fn)` redraws when a picture or font arrives. Colors only via theme tokens.
- 3D cabinets: `cabinet3d` in `games.js` (`profile`, `trim`, `trim2`, `body`; all optional, defaults come from the 2D `cabinet`, so the two views always match). `arcade3d.js` holds `PROFILES` (side-view points extruded into the body, with anchors for marquee, screen, panel, door, START and a topper: `peak`/`dome`/`fins`/`pagoda`/`vault`/`gate`/`vs`/`puck`/`lamp`/`wing`/`dish`/`lanterns`/`scroll`; `dial: true` = a round safe door; `twoPlayer: true` = two joysticks and two sets of buttons; `wheel: true` = a steering wheel and gear stick), the canvas-texture marquees and attract screens (canvas versions of `SCREENS`, same loops), and the scene. `arcade.js` owns all controls and HTML; a view (2D or 3D) implements `place(cur, instant)`, `pick(event)`, `startLink`, `destroy()`. The 3D canvas is decoration (`aria-hidden`); START is a real `<a>` positioned over the front cabinet, and it does not follow the sway.
- 3D performance rules: three.js loads only when a zone opens (never for the lobby / ALL GAMES); while it loads no 2D cabinets are built (only if 3D gives up); in a zone only the front cabinet and its neighbors (within `NEAR` = 1.5 places of where the carousel is and where it's going) are FULL cabinets, farther ones a FLAT stand-in (one textured plane: silhouette + marquee + screen, `flatCab`), swapped as it turns, and cabinets beyond `VIS` (2.6 places, never visible) have nothing built; a FAST SPIN (more than `SPIN_FROM` 2.5 places, `SPIN_MS` 650: the Full Arcade's jump strip) builds full cabinets only at both ends, the ones it flies past get a flat stand-in as they come into view; `setRing(ring, wrap, cur, fade)` swaps zones and `setRing([])` (the lobby) disposes every cabinet's geometry, materials and the zone's marquee/screen textures (the renderer stays, paused by `A.floorPaused`); `stats()` adds `built`, `disposed`, `full`, `flat`. Render only while something moves (turn, sway, front attract screen), ≤ 30 fps when idle, stop when the tab is hidden; pixel ratio ≤ 1.5; no post-processing (glow = additive planes and fat translucent tubes; reflection = the ring drawn upside down under a see-through floor, without small parts or far cabinets); average frame > 40 ms over 3 s → pixel ratio 1, no haze, no sway; still slow → 2D. `Arcade.Floor3D.stats()` reports frame times and draw calls. Colors come from the theme tokens (`getComputedStyle`), never hard-coded.
- The arcade floor look: a dark 1980s arcade at night: haze, neon sign (Monoton, `--neon-font`; its size is `--logo` in arcade.css, and `--cab-w` leaves room for it, so a bigger sign shrinks the cabinets instead of pushing START down), cabinets glowing down an aisle with light pooling on the floor. How visible the checkered floor is = `--floor-checks` at the top of arcade.css (0 = no checks, 1 = the strongest; default .55), read by both the 3D floor (arcade3d.js) and the 2D one. Carousel turns stay under 0.5 s; only the front cabinet's screen animates; `prefers-reduced-motion` gives a plain fade and no attract loops. At phone width only one cabinet shows, arrows beside it. Nothing may scroll sideways.
- OVERLAYS SCROLL THEMSELVES: theme.css `.overlay` is the scroll container (`overflow-y: auto`, `overscroll-behavior: contain`, `touch-action: pan-y`, iOS momentum); its panel is centered by auto margins and starts at the top when taller than the screen. Never give an overlay's panel `max-height` + `overflow: hidden`, and never let it be cut off: let the overlay scroll. For a long overlay that must keep the page behind still, call `Arcade.lockScroll(true)` on open and `lockScroll(false)` on close (ui.js). Arcade Quest's `.q-panel` scrolls inside the game screen (`touch-action: pan-y` because `#stage` takes over touch).
- Keep the neon arcade look: a near-black arcade floor (`--floor`, `--floor-2`, `--floor-3`, `--deep`) lit by neon `--pink`, `--cyan` and `--yellow` (each with `-hi`/`-ink` variants and a `--glow-*` shadow). Every color is a token in `shared/theme.css` (cabinets also use `--purple`, `--amber`, `--green` and the `--cab-*` woodwork tokens); don't hard-code colors in a game. Neon is for titles, borders, glows and short labels; long text uses `--text-hi`/`--text-lo` so it meets WCAG AA. The staff `.stage` is a light "screen" (`--screen`, `--ink`) in a cyan neon frame, so notation stays crisp. Titles use the display font (`--display`, Bungee); body text stays Atkinson Hyperlegible (`--text`). Marquee fonts (`GN Haunt`, `GN Pixel`, `GN Shade`, `GN Neon`, `GN Brush` = Kaushan Script, Vanishing Ink's sign and titles) are subset .woff2 files in `shared/fonts/` covering only Latin letters, digits and a little punctuation: signs only, never body text. Keep motion light (no blur filters) and turn it off under `prefers-reduced-motion`. The older names (`--gold`, `--night-2`, `--bone`, `--mist`, `--coral`…) are aliases of the new tokens.

## Note Storm (`note-storm/`)
- THE UI KIT (every game below uses it the same way, see Engine API): results = `A.UI.results.show`, the pause = `A.UI.pause` (active only while a level/round/match runs; the in-level "Quit"/"Leave" buttons are gone: pause → BACK TO … asks first), intros = `A.UI.intro`, option toggles = `.ui-seg`. Note Storm's pause stops the loop (Tempo's `.is-paused`); BACK TO LEVELS in Endless drops the run ("End this run?"), as Quit run did.
- Notes march left along the staff toward Tempo (the robot); play a note to blast it; one reaching Tempo costs a life (3). LISTENS (pitch.js). NOTES × ORDER like every note-reading game.
- SEVERAL NOTES AT ONCE: a played pitch blasts the note CLOSEST TO TEMPO with that pitch (`G.notes.find`, oldest first); no match on the staff = a wrong note. One held note = one blast (onHeld fires once per note): the same pitch twice needs a fresh attack; the first multi-note level shows "Same note twice? Tongue it again!" once per device (`gameData('note-storm').sameTip`). New notes wait for `maxOn` and `RULES.gapUnits` of room, so they never overlap; the staff gets wider (a smaller drawing on phones) so `maxOn` notes fit.
- THE STORM NEVER STOPS: notes, spawning and the clock keep going through shots, sounds and the mic's mute; only the pause button, a hidden tab and the start/results screens stop it. The first note waits for the `level-start` sound. `RULES.muteMs` = `Sfx.muteMax`.
- `levels.js` = `STORM_LEVELS` (8 levels, one commented table: count, pool, march (s to reach Tempo; keep ≥ 2.5), every (average s between notes; keep ≥ 0.7), gust (runs of quick then slow notes, same average), maxOn 1–5, names) + `STORM_RULES` + `STORM_ENDLESS`. Stars saved by level number: keep 8 levels. `A.Storm.state()` for tests.
- ENDLESS (`G.endless`, the ∞ card): `G.L` is recomputed every frame from SPEED (1 = Level 1's pace): march = march1 ÷ SPEED (never under `minMarch` 2.2 s), every = every ÷ SPEED (never under `minEvery`), maxOn from `more` (5 at about Level 8's speed); past the march floor the rising SPEED only brings notes closer together. Notes come in 24-note chunks from `ModePicker.sequence` (the smaller pool until `smallPoolUntil`; a chunk never starts on the last note's pitch). The staff is laid out for 5 notes from the start (`G.roomOn`). A note reaching Tempo = a heart (`endless-life-lost`); a wrong note only breaks the combo. The HUD shows Speed, Combo, hearts, Score.

## Keys to the City (`keys-to-the-city/`)
- THE UI KIT: the intro is `UI.intro` (the Mayor, the mini keyboard, LET'S GO! / CITY MAP); the kit pause freezes the round clock (`G.t0` shifted) and the wait between rounds (`later()`); BACK TO THE CITY MAP ends a Night Shift with keys right as SHIFT OVER; results hero = the golden key; the finale shows over the results (z 65) and announces after its button.
- Piano-keyboard note reading in a neon city at night (zone note-reading). The keyboard is the SKYLINE: a real piano layout (white keys, black keys in groups of 2 and 3, real width ratios, black keys `BHK` 0.63 of the white length, off-center in their groups like a real piano; on short screens the keys get SHORTER, never narrower than `RULES.minKeyPx` 46 px), black keys drawn as skyscrapers with windows and a rooftop, white keys as lit streets. A key being asked about switches its windows on (`.lit`); a right answer lights its block gently (`lightBlock`: the C–E block or the F–B block, one soft fade); wrong = coral outline, the right key gold. (No Main St lamp any more: nothing marks middle C.) C/F PLATES (levels.js `labels`, like `signs`: 1, [from, to] or none): a small dark sign with "C" / "F" in the Chopsticks' / the Fork's color at the bottom of every C and F key (`.kt-cf`, `setLabels`): Downtown C on, Restaurant Row fading to 0 with the signs, none from Sharp Street on; SHOW SIGNS brings the plates back too. The keyboard covers every clef the district uses and PANS (0.45 s, none under reduced motion) to the asked keys; FIND THE KEY pans to a random spot so the key's position never gives it away.
- THE BLACK-KEY GROUPS HAVE EXACTLY TWO NAMES, EVERYWHERE (hints, the Mayor, help text, docs): THE CHOPSTICKS (2 black keys; "C is right next to the Chopsticks") and THE FORK (3 black keys; "F is right next to the Fork"). Neon line-drawing signs (`signSVG`) over EVERY group on the keyboard, bright on early districts, fading by `signs` ([from, to] across a district), SHOW SIGNS brings them back for one round (−`signsCost` points). TAP A SIGN (`signTap`): while a sign is visible (brightness ≥ `RULES.signTapFrom` .2: the `.tap` class; a big invisible `.sg-hit` area in the sky over its group that stops above the black keys) a tap plays its hint `kttc-mayor-chopsticks` / `-fork` (mic: true, so instrument mode mutes the detector while it speaks) and the Mayor's bubble shows the words; never an answer, never costs points; the same hint never restarts or stacks, the other sign's hint stops it (`Sfx.hush`); the wrong-answer sign hints go through the same `hintVoice`. THE LEVEL INTRO shows the MINI KEYBOARD (`miniKeyboard()`: one octave C–C, the two signs over their groups with dashed arrows to C and F, lit in the signs' colors, "C is right next to the Chopsticks" / "F is right next to the Fork") on every district with signs (not The Mayor's Challenge). Never any other name for them, and no cultural fonts or imagery.
- `levels.js` = `KTTC_LEVELS` (the CITY MAP, 9 districts: Downtown C, Restaurant Row, Sharp Street, Flat Iron District, Ledger Line Heights, Bass Clef Harbor, Key Signature Square, Sharp Signature Summit, The Mayor's Challenge; saved by number: never reorder), `KTTC_RULES` (stars 70/85/100 % of rounds right, points, timings, `minKeyPx`), `KTTC_NIGHT` (the endless). TIMED ROUNDS (The Mayor's Challenge, Night Shift): base time + `RULES.perTap` 1.5 s for every tap after the first (a black key's name, FULL CIRCUIT's placing: `tapsFor`); SCALE BUILDER is never timed (only Key Signature Square and Sharp Signature Summit). THE QUESTION STAFF (game.js `staffLayout` / `drawStaff` / `sizeStaff`): laid out from the DISTRICT, never the round's note (so nothing jumps): width = the district's widest key signature + the open space (`OPEN`, `SCALE_OPEN` for Scale Builder), height = the district's range in that clef (stems, ledger lines, a ♯ under the lowest note, the clef; Scale Builder up to a 4th above the range) with even padding (`VPAD`); the note sits in the middle of the open space after the clef and key signature (`noteX`: a ♯/♭ counts as part of it), and Full Circuit's placing note uses the same x. Sized in px as large as the width, `STAFF_MAXH` (27 % of the screen height) and the room above the keyboard at its smallest (`KB_MIN`) allow, never smaller than the old staff (`OLD_MAXH`, `oldHeight`); the white box hugs the drawing and the space under it (`#stage` margin) makes up the district's tallest layout, so the keyboard never moves; a round that doesn't use the letter pad (Find the Key, Scale Builder) lends the pad's space to the staff (`STAFF_MAXH_LENT`, the pad `display: none`). The pad is reserved in the shape the district uses (♭ ♮ ♯ only where black keys or key signatures come). The key-signature hint text (`#keyName`) sits at the top of the box. shared/ui.js `staffSVG` takes `box: [top, height]` and `capY` for this. `quiz.js` (`Arcade.KTTC`, testable alone) = the music model (concert = piano) and the round maker: FIND THE KEY (octave-exact), NAME THE KEY (AnswerPad), FULL CIRCUIT (name it, then place it on the staff: a finger moves a note over the lines and spaces, letting go places it, ledger lines drawn), SCALE BUILDER (8 keys up from the tonic, in order). SPELLING (`nameOk`): no key signature = a white key's plain letter, a black key either name unless the district says `sharps` / `flats` (`flats-any` = flats, then either); with a key signature (F, B♭, E♭, A♭, G, D, A) only the key's own spelling, and staff notes mean the altered key (a B in F major is B♭); `sigHint` rounds light the key signature's ♯/♭ that applies. Early districts' wrong answers show the sign hint (`signHint`).
- TOUCH mode (default, no instrument, no microphone, `player: 'all'`): taps play `Sfx.piano`. INSTRUMENT mode (the level screen's toggle; needs a pitched saved instrument, else the floor's pick mode `index.html?pick=keys-to-the-city`): FIND THE KEY is answered by playing the concert pitch (`Pitch.onHeld`, any octave); `helper` shows "Piano B♭ = your C" (the member's written note, `sounds`); NO piano or other pitched sound plays (every effect is unpitched noise, mic: true, short). Name/circuit/scale rounds stay touch. Stars count in both modes: `setLevel('keys-to-the-city', 'all', district, {stars, best})`, the best kept. Clef choice Treble / Bass / Both (default: the saved instrument's clef) for districts with `clefs: 'pref'`.
- NIGHT SHIFT (the Endless tile): stages every `every` right answers add keys, clefs, key signatures, less time per round (`time`), 3 lives, Top 5 per device (`instKey 'all'`, `setKey 'night-shift'`; not saved in `?demo`). THE MAYOR: an original cat in a top hat and sash (`mayorSVG`, `--kt-fur*` tokens); gives a golden key for each district (the icon on its card), and clearing The Mayor's Challenge sets `gameData.achievements.mayor` = the Golden City Key hand item + the City Skyline background (bg scene `keys-city`) + the KEYS TO THE CITY finale (`kttc-keys-to-city`).
- Sounds (screen `keys-to-the-city`, all unpitched): `keys-to-the-city-menu`, `kttc-window-on`, `kttc-correct`, `kttc-wrong`, `kttc-block-lights` (every 5 in a row), `kttc-golden-key`, `kttc-keys-to-city`, optional voice slots `kttc-mayor-hello` (silent until recorded, never while listening) and `kttc-mayor-chopsticks/-fork` (the sign hints; they may play while listening). Cabinet `shape: 'keys'` (a skyline over the marquee, the two signs painted on the sides, a real little keyboard on the kick plate), `screen: 'keys'`, marquee scene `keys` (GN Neon), 3D profile `keys` with a `skyline` topper, menu background `keys-city`. Tokens `--kt-*`. `?demo`: every district open, Space = the right answer, W = a wrong one (instrument mode: hold Space to "play" it); `Arcade.KeysCity.state()` (incl. `labels`, `signTap`, `limit`, `hint`) / `signTap(kind)` / `answer()` / `tapKey(midi)` / `name(letter, acc)` / `place(step)` / `begin(lv | 'endless')` for tests.

## Music Highway (`music-highway/`)
- Play-along rhythm game (Technique Lab): neon LIGHT PADS race down a perspective synthwave highway (one canvas: `proj(dt)`: depth d = (1 + `roadDepth`)^(dt ÷ lead), so a pad's size and its distance to the next shrink together) to their lane's GATE on the strike line in time with a backing groove; a scrolling staff underneath on its own dark panel (written pitch, the student's clef + key signature pinned on the left, letter names, a playhead; hit notes gold, missed coral). LISTENS (pitch.js + mic-gate.js). `unpitched: true` (the snare plays each song's rhythm in two STICKING lanes, L left / R right; judging stays timing-only: any clean attack), `byMember: true`: `setLevel('music-highway', member id, song number, {stars, best: score})`; `maxStars` 141 (47 songs × 3). SPEED: SLOW 75 % (no stars) | NORMAL | TURBO 125 % (`turboRate`; stars like Normal, and a star on Turbo earns that song's ⚡ TURBO badge on the song list: `gameData.turbo[member][song id]`); judging windows stay the same in ms at every speed. The best stars and score are kept (`setLevel` gets the max). PRACTICE THIS PART (the trouble-spot loop) gives no stars. MODE: PLAY | PRACTICE (`gameData.mode`): PRACTICE never calls `requireMic` (no prompt, no pitch detection, nothing judged, scored or saved); the full pitched band plays out loud (the headphones voices at `practiceMelodyVol`/`practiceBassVol`/`practicePadVol`; MELODY On/Off + a MELODY VOLUME slider, `gameData.melody`/`melVol`; the snare hears the song's concert melody + chords and the full drums), each note's gate lights as it sounds (`res: 'guide'`), the HUD says "PRACTICE: not scored", and the results say "Practice complete!" with PLAY THIS SONG / PRACTICE AGAIN; pause, restart, song menu and every speed work. Unlocks: tier 2 = stars on any 3 tier-1 songs, tier 3 = stars on any 3 tier-2 songs, tier 4 (THE FINAL BOSS) = stars on any 3 tier-3 songs (per member; `?demo` all).
- THE FINAL BOSS (tier 4; song 47, "Flight of the Bumblebee", written A minor for trumpet = concert G minor, `key: 'Bb', mode: 'minor'`; 57 notes, all sixteenths but the last; ♩ = 72, TURBO 90 = 6 notes a second): lanes and octave like tier 3 (`MAX_LANES[4]` 12; it spans 11 pitches, so no member merges a lane), the snare plays it in its two sticking lanes; judging, speeds and stars as every song. The song select shows it in its own FINAL BOSS section at the bottom (`#bossHead`, `.mh-boss` gold card); locked = a buzzing bee silhouette + "Earn stars on 3 tier-3 songs to face the Bumblebee." (the level select's lock line). Its words live in game.js `BOSS` (a tier-4 song without an entry gets generic ones). VISUAL ONLY, judging never changes: THE INTRO CARD (`UI.intro`, theme `mh-boss-intro`: "FINAL BOSS: FLIGHT OF THE BUMBLEBEE" / "Sixteenth notes all the way. Keep your fingers light and your air steady!" / READY) the first time each play session (`Arcade.session` 'mh-boss-intro'), before the microphone, the timing check and the count-in; its sting `mh-boss-intro` (mic: false, falls back to `special-alert`) plays as the card opens and READY waits for it to end. THE SWARM (game.js `makeSwarm`/`drawSwarm`/`swarmReact`, `BEE`): 6 original cartoon bees (one sprite drawn once; tokens `--mh-bee`, `--mh-bee-stripe`, `--mh-bee-wing`) zig-zag slowly just above the horizon; a hit = the nearest bee does a happy loop, a miss = one buzzes past that gate; still and unreacting under reduced motion, none on 'lo' effects. BADGES on the song card: 🐝 BUMBLEBEE TAMER = any star; 👑 BUMBLEBEE LEGEND = 3 ★ on TURBO (`gameData.legend[member][song id]`). AVATAR UNLOCKS (avatar-parts.js, never sold): the Bumblebee pet = a star on song 47 (`{game, level: 47, stars: 1}`), Bee Wings (back) = `gameData('music-highway').achievements['bumblebee-legend']` (set by the first LEGEND). `Arcade.Highway.begin(i)` / `boss()` (intro count, swarm) for tests: tests/music-highway-boss.spec.js.
- `songs.js` = THE SONG LIST (Mat edits; songs are levels: NEVER reorder or remove, add at the end; 47 songs, 27–46 = Mr. Graham's transcriptions from trumpet parts, in method-book order, 47 = THE FINAL BOSS): `{id, title, source, tier, tempo, timeSig, key: 'Bb' (concert major), mode?: 'minor', style: rock|march|swing|waltz, chords?, notes: [{deg, oct, beats, acc?} | {rest}]}`. WRITTEN (trumpet) KEY → CONCERT: C major = 'Bb', F major = 'Eb', G major = 'F', B♭ major = 'Ab', D minor = 'Eb' + minor, A minor = 'Bb' + minor, E minor = 'F' + minor; repeats, endings and D.C. al Fine are written out. METERS (`timeSig`): [4, 4], [3, 4], [2, 4], [2, 2], [6, 8], [3, 8]; beats are always QUARTER notes (`tempo` = quarters a minute; 6/8 = 3 beats a measure, 3/8 = 1.5) and everything follows the PRIMARY beat = `SongMap.meter(song)` `{per, pulse (1; 2/2: 2; 6/8 and 3/8: 1.5), pulses, countBars, countClicks, countBeats, label}`: the COUNT-IN (a quarter-beat meter: one measure; 2/2 two bars of half-note clicks, 6/8 two bars of dotted-quarter clicks, 3/8 four bars of one: always 4 clicks, each bar's downbeat the strong click; RESUME counts in the same way), the GROOVES (backing.js `groove(style, timeSig)`: 2/2 = a march in 2: kick 1, snare 3, hats on the quarters; 6/8 = kick 1, snare 4 (beat 1.5), hats on every eighth, 1 and 4 stronger; 3/8 = kick 1, soft hats on the three eighths, no snare; 3/4 always the waltz), the highway's cross lines (one per primary beat, downbeats magenta: `drawRoad({pulse})`), the chords' weights and the snare's DOWNBEATS RIGHT (R on a primary beat); the song select and the Song Board print the meter ('¢ 2/2'). The notes are typed as NOTE TEXT through `N()` (bar lines checked; A `|` AFTER EVERY MEASURE (each string also ends one): `check` reports any stretch longer than one measure with no bar line ("<id>: measure N has no bar line after it (add | so each measure is checked)"), because a wrong rhythm can hide inside it (The Entertainer's missing sixteenth rest on beat 2 of measures 3/7/11 did, fixed: 13 measures, tests/music-highway-entertainer.spec.js, which also checks every other song's events against tests/fixtures/mh-song-events.json); `5:2~` = TIED into the next, same note: ONE held note in the game, one pad and trail, each side counted in its own measure, drawn as a tie; `(3 4 5)` = a SLUR (may cross bar lines, may hold a tie; an unclosed `(`, a stray `)`, a slur in a slur, a rest inside or a one-note slur are reported by `check`); `key: 'Eb'` = written F major for trumpet).
