Format + future MIDI importer: `music-highway/README.md`. `song-map.js` (`Arcade.SongMap`): degrees → concert (B♭3 = degree 1) → each member's WRITTEN notes (`sounds`), spelled in its written key, octave: tier 1 = degree 1 on the group's first-five tonic, else the best fit in the GMEA range, and THE RANGE RULE: a tier 2–3 note that octave still leaves outside the member's GMEA range moves by an octave into it (`folded`), so every instrument plays every note (the oboe's D6 in The Merry Minstrels / March of the Toreadors, the tenor sax's B3, the F horn's E3 and G5); a group whose first five start elsewhere (the C–G horn) gets the song moved to its first five (concert F); `check(song)` reports measures that don't add up (console + Song Board); chords per measure (given, else I/IV/V from the melody).
- LANES = PITCHES (`SongMap.lanes(song, map, group)`): one lane per written pitch, lowest on the left, so the melody's shape shows on the road; tier 1 = exactly the group's first five (all five even if a song skips one), tier 2 ≤ 8, tier 3 ≤ 12 (`MAX_LANES`: past that the least-used pitch joins its nearest neighbor's lane, order kept; a lane of two pitches is labeled "E/F"); SNARE = two sticking lanes, L (left hand) and R (right hand): each pad in the lane of its hand (`lanes.of(n)` for every instrument; `?demo` logs a sticking self-check of the first two measures, `Arcade.Highway.stickCheck()`). STICKING (song-map.js `stickings`): the song's own `sticking` field ('RLRL RRLL…', one letter per note; `check` reports a wrong count) else the student's pattern on the song select (`gameData.sticking`, snare only, with "Follow the sticking! The lanes show which hand to use."): ALTERNATE (default: hand to hand, every measure starts with R, rests don't count) | DOWNBEATS RIGHT (on a beat R, off the beat L); a long note takes the hand its rule gives. The snare's staff is the one-line percussion staff with R/L under the notes. Each lane has a neon GATE with the lane's written letter name under it. PADS: rounded neon tail lights in the pitch's color (`--mh-c`…`--mh-b`), dark outline, the letter inside unless LETTER NAMES: OFF (the song select, `gameData.names`; the gates keep their names; stars count either way). FULL-VALUE TRAILS: every note of `trailFrom` (1: a quarter) beats or more (not the snare) trails a light for its length, ending `trailGap` (12 %) of it early (`n.tEnd`) so notes stay apart; it burns bright while held (`n.holding`). Held to `fullValueShare` (85 %; a note still sounding at its trail's end counts as held to there) = FULL VALUE (a quiet pop-up `#fv`; +`fullValuePoints` for quarter and dotted-quarter notes; long notes (≥ `holdFrom`) keep their per-beat hold bonus); the results show "Full value held" (%). Eighths and shorter: no trail. No fingering diagrams any more (fingerings.js + diagrams.js are still loaded only because song-map.js's octave fit prefers notes that have a fingering, so every song keeps its notes).
- THE BACKDROP (bg-scenes.js `S.sunset.paint`, shared with the menu scene `highway`): purple-to-pink sky, a few stars, a big striped sun on the horizon, wireframe mountains, the ground grid to the vanishing point; drawn ONCE into an offscreen canvas (`V.bg`, with the road, lanes and unlit gates); ONLY THE ROAD MOVES: the sky, stars, sun, mountains and the ground beside the road never change during a song (no sun swell); each frame draws only, ON the road, one cross line per beat (crossing the gates ON the beat; downbeats magenta) and the lane dividers' dashes rolling with them, plus trails, pads and gates; reduced motion = the road stands still too. The menu scene keeps its own gentle motion. PERFORMANCE: `perfWatch` averages draw time and frame gaps over 180 frames; over `fxSlowDrawMs` / `fxSlowGapMs` = effects 'lo' for good on this device (`gameData.fx`: 15 stars, no mountains, no halos, pixel ratio 1). Measured 54–58 fps at 4× CPU throttle (stays 'hi').
- THE DRAWING (`shared/highway-draw.js`, `Arcade.HighwayDraw`, loaded by the game and the floor page before cabinets.js): `paintStatic` (sunset, sky, gates' labels: painted once), `drawRoad`, `drawTrails`, `drawPads`, `drawGates`, `proj`/`laneX` for a view `V`; the game and the cabinet's attract demo both draw with it, so a look change reaches both. Never copy it into another file.
- THE STAFF IS REAL NOTATION (`music-highway/notation.js`, `Arcade.MHNotation.engrave`; also the trouble spot and the Song Board): real note values (whole … sixteenth, dotted), rests (not dotted except 6/8; a whole measure = a whole rest), ties across bar lines and awkward beats, beams by group (4/4 and 2/2 half a measure, 2/4 and 3/4 a beat, 6/8 three eighths, 3/8 the whole measure), 2/2 drawn ¢, dotted rests only in 6/8 / 3/8 (a dotted-quarter rest on a beat), a note across the middle of a 6/8 bar tied there like the book, stems up below the middle line / down on or above it (a beam follows its group's majority), accidentals that last to the bar line (♮ when needed), bar lines, a time signature, the written key signature. SPACING like printed music (settings.js `staff`): a fixed pad after/before every bar line, space = noteBase × (1 + noteGrow × log2(length ÷ the song's shortest)), never under noteMin, + room for accidentals and dots; never positioned by time. The staff scrolls by the engraving's TIME → X MAP (a monotone curve through every note/rest onset, `xAt(beats)`), so each notehead reaches the playhead exactly when its pad reaches its gate (measured ≤ 1 ms over whole songs); its speed changes a little, the pads' doesn't. Groups carry `data-x`; `.halo` = the lit-note glow.
- PAD SPACING (settings.js): two pads in a row are at least `cardGap` (40 %) of a pad apart at the gates, even for the song's quickest note step, and the road's perspective keeps that true all the way up (`spacing()` in game.js picks the lead time; too fast to read (< `readMinS`) = smaller pads for that song, never under `cardMinScale`; pads NEVER overlap). NOTE SPACING Normal | Wide (`wideMul` 1.5; `gameData.wide`, stars count in both). `Arcade.Highway.spacing()` / `cardRects(t)` (pad boxes) / `lanes()` / `trace()` + `traceLog()` (what each frame drew) / `fx()` for tests.
- THE GATE GLOW (visual only): PERFECT / GOOD / OK lights that lane's GATE (canvas, `GL.lanes`) in the pad's color, plus the staff note (`.lit`); the judgment pops up above the gate (`#judge`). A long note stays lit while held (`n.holding`, the hold-bonus check; its trail burns bright too) and fades when let go; a short one `glowLingerMs` past its end, so runs stay lit. Fades only (in 60 ms, out 200 ms), a gate goes out at most once per `glowMinCycleMs` (≤ 3 flashes a second); reduced motion = on/off with no fades. Miss / wrong note = a dim red gate outline (`badMs`). `Arcade.Highway.glow()` for tests.
- PAUSE = THE UI KIT's pause (`A.UI.pause`, `pauseOnBlur`: Esc / P, a hidden tab, `pagehide`, or the window unfocused 0.6 s; the button top-left above `#play`, whose HUD `layout()` moves past it; kit panels are z-index 60+ above `#play` (40)) with the score and accuracy so far: RESUME / RESTART / SETTINGS (NOTE SPACING and LETTER NAMES are registered there: letter names at once, a new spacing when the song resumes) / SONG MENU (`levelsLabel`; asks first only when a score would be lost). Pausing stops the scheduler + `kit.stopAll()` (drums, clicks, the headphones band), freezes the song clock (`songNow()` = `G.pausedRaw`) and the drawing, drops pending attacks and holds; nothing heard counts; no menu music. RESUME = back ONE MEASURE from the beat it stopped on (never before 0), a one-measure count-in, then on in step; notes already judged keep their result. SONG MENU = `showHub()` at once: no results, no stars, nothing saved, the song still selected. Practice loops pause the same way. `Arcade.Highway.pauses()` / `paused()` for tests (measured: 5 pauses in one song incl. the count-in, a held note and the end, drift ≤ 0.8 ms, no note judged twice).
- THE CLOCK: the arcade's AudioContext (`Sfx.output()`, read through `Arcade.AudioClock` in shared/calibration.js); drums scheduled on it `lookaheadS` ahead (backing.js `Arcade.MHBacking`: noise snare/hat/clicks + a 70 ms kick, grooves per style, no toms); the highway, staff and judge read the AUDIBLE time (`getOutputTimestamp` / `outputLatency`, smoothed), so nothing drifts (measured: 136 s at 4× CPU throttle, every note within 1 ms). Sound off = performance.now(). A one-measure count-in (clicks + hat + kick). THE CLICK (count-in + calibration): sounds.js `mh-click` (Mat's recording) else backing.js's generated woodblock (noise bands, no pitch, 55 ms, ~75 % of its energy 1.5–4 kHz, rendered once and normalized to peak 1; the downbeat ~15 % higher and louder, the others `CLICK.weak` 72 %); `kit.click()` returns its peak and the count-in's hat and kick stay in the headroom left, so `clickVol` 1 never clips. The count-in follows the EFFECTS slider; the calibration clicks use `Sfx.outputRaw()` (no EFFECTS slider, SOUND ON/OFF still mutes). `shared/sounds/mh-drums-<song id>.m4a` (optional; sounds.js makes the event for every song) replaces the generated drums (normal speed, pitched instruments). (Pause: see PAUSE above.)
- SLURS (winds only: the snare ignores them, the bells get no feedback): events carry `slur` (its group), `slurFirst`, `slurLast` (song-map.js); notation.js draws one arc per slur OPPOSITE THE STEMS (all stems up = under the heads, else over them; a row break runs it to the edge); the highway joins each slurred pad to the next with a thin ribbon (`HighwayDraw.drawSlurs`, `n.slurTo`). JUDGING NEVER CHANGES for a slur. FEEDBACK ONLY: a slurred note after the first of its slur arriving with a CLEAR new attack (the attack's `rise` ≥ `slurTongueRise` 3, within `slurAtkMs`) = "tongued", a smooth pitch change (the soft-entry path or a weaker attack) = SMOOTH, a small pop under the judgment; after the song ONE tip when more than `slurTipShare` of the slurred notes were tongued, for the slur tongued the most ("Measure 3: slur it! One air stream, move only your fingers."; trombone: "Keep the air moving through the slide change; a light 'doo' is OK.", `#slurTip`). SLUR TIPS On/Off in the Settings panel (`gameData.slurTips`, default On) hides the pops and the tip. `Arcade.Highway.slurLog()` / `slurTip()` / `slurLinks()` / `results()`, `autoPlay(ms, {slur: 'tongue' | 'smooth'})`; tests/music-highway-slurs.spec.js.
- JUDGING (`settings.js` MH_RULES): by the note's ATTACK (`Pitch.onAttack`) after the latency correction, confirmed by the right pitch class within `pitchConfirmMs` (the attack's own pitch, else the next readings); a note that starts with no attack heard (a very soft entry) counts from its first steady reading (`softEntryMs`); the closest open note with that pitch wins. PERFECT ±90 · GOOD ±170 · OK ±260 · EARLY/LATE to ±420 (counts, combo resets) · else MISS; wrong pitch = "That's D" (visual only). Score ×1–×4 by combo, hold bonus per beat held (letting go ends only the bonus). Accuracy = average of `value`; stars at 60/80/95 %. Results: counts, max combo, the TROUBLE SPOT (the 2 measures with the most misses, on a mini staff) + PRACTICE THIS PART (loops them at 60 %, `practiceLoops`). No sound effects during a song (results use the shared events).
- SNARE vs ITS OWN DRUMS: `onAttack` gets `level` (pitch.js); an attack within `bleedMs` of one of the backing's hits with no note near it (the count-in, between notes) teaches the bleed level; on a note such an attack counts only above `bleedK` × that level; the backing leaves out its own snare for a snare player. Verified with the game's output looped straight into the "microphone": trumpet, tuba and snare score 0 hits when nobody plays.
- CALIBRATION ("Play any note on each of the 8 clicks", after 4 to listen to): the CLICK NEVER COUNTS: a pitched instrument's attack needs a pitch; the snare's must be `bleedK` × louder than the attacks heard on the 4 listening clicks; a round whose offsets are machine-steady (< `calMinSpreadMs`) within `calClickMaxMs` of 0, or earlier than −`calTooEarlyMs`, is refused with "I heard the click, not your instrument. Try playing a little louder or moving the device a bit farther from you." (verified with the game's output looped into the microphone: 0 rounds accepted for trumpet, tuba, snare). Median offset of attacks to clicks, saved per device for speaker and headphones mode separately (`gameData('music-highway').calib`); THE FIRST SONG OF EVERY PLAY SESSION (per mode, `Arcade.session`) starts with it even on a calibrated device (done or skipped, it isn't forced again until a reload; a device never calibrated is still asked each song); RECALIBRATE on the song select any time. HEADPHONES MODE ("🎧 I'm wearing headphones", remembered): the SPEAKER CHECK plays three test notes while listening; heard (≥ `checkFrames` readings of 2 of them) = "It sounds like your speakers are on. Plug in headphones to use this mode." and it stays off; it runs each time the mode is turned on and before the first song of a page load with it on.
- The Song Board (`music-highway/songs.html`, linked from the song select and the Sound Board): every song in concert pitch and for any member (horn both starts) as real notation (notation.js, rows justified), letter names per measure, tempo/tier/source, ▶ PLAY (soft synth + the groove, no mic). Cabinet: `shape: 'highway'` (a highway painted down the front, a strike line across the panel), `screen: 'highway'` = THE ATTRACT DEMO (`Arcade.HighwayDraw.attract(ctx, W, H, t)`, a canvas screen: the still sunset, 5 lanes C–G, pads + full-value trails playing Hot Cross Buns' first phrase (`HighwayDraw.DEMO`), gates fading in/out, PERFECT/GOOD, COMBO; `t = null` = the still frame `DEMO.still`; no staff strip: unreadable at cabinet size; no sound), marquee scene `highway` (a neon road into a starfield with neon pads, GN Neon), 3D profile `highway` with a `road` topper (a lit highway sign: road + stars, no icon), menu background scene `highway` (the game's own sunset art). Avatar reward: the Neon Highway background (a star on The Entertainer). `?demo`: Space/T = the note at the line, W wrong, hold S, 1–5 = first five; `Arcade.Highway.state()` / `autoPlay(offsetMs | [offsets])` / `start(i, {practice})` / `speakerResult()` / `clock()` / `meter()` (the count-in clicks and drum hits in beats) for tests; the meters and the book songs: tests/music-highway-meters.spec.js (notation.js `engrave(…).layout()` = every piece + the beamed groups).

## Rhythm Dojo (`rhythm-dojo/`)
- Rhythm reading (zone ninja-dojo; `player: 'all'`, `unpitched: true`, no instrument needed; `maxStars` 33 = 11 levels × 3; progress `setLevel('rhythm-dojo', 'all', level, {stars, best: score})`). A rhythm on the ONE-LINE STAFF with MR. GRAHAM'S COUNTING underneath (shared/rhythm-staff.js + counting.js: RHYTHMS AND COUNTING above); the student PERFORMS it: TAP (THE DEFAULT on a device with no saved mode, first of the mode buttons and pressed in the markup: a big drum pad or Space, no microphone: a tap counts on pointerdown / Space keydown; letting go never matters; its timing check is the tap one, so nothing asks for the microphone until CLAP or SNARE is picked), CLAP (the microphone, shared/onsets.js: claps as onsets, never pitch), or SNARE (the microphone, like CLAP). NOTHING IS HELD in any mode (claps, taps and snare hits can't sustain): every note is judged only by when it starts; students SAY the small underlined counts of a long note (never clap/tap them), and no text may tell them to hold a note. `gameData('rhythm-dojo')` = `{mode, counting, speed, hp, calib: {clap, tap}}` (the snare uses clap's timing check).
- A ROUND: the rhythm shows (study) → HEAR IT (optional, the microphone PAUSED: the woodblock (`kit.block`) over the click, a playhead, each syllable lighting as it sounds) → PERFORM: `rd-count-in` ("Ready!") ends, then a ONE-MEASURE COUNT-IN (clicks + the big beat number ON THE TAIKO's barrel (`#countBig`, aria-live, sized to the drum: `78cqh` of its box, a clamp fallback) + a row of beat dots under it (`#countDots`, one lit per beat: 4, 3 in 3/4, 6 in 6/8); NEVER over the notation; 6/8 counts all six), the playhead and THE PULSE LIGHT (the taiko's rim: brightest on each beat, fading smoothly: 1 swell a beat, ≤ 2.2 a second; steady with reduced motion). CLAP/SNARE: no clicks after the count-in (HEADPHONES mode, after the headphones check proved the microphone can't hear the clicks, keeps the beat going); TAP: the beat keeps going and each tap knocks the woodblock. EVERY ROW ON SCREEN: `fitRows()` scales the rows (all at one scale) until the sheet fits between the dojo and the bottom of the screen, never below `FIT_MIN` 0.6 px per staff unit (the small syllables ≥ 11 px); a pass scrolls the dojo + sheet into view (`inView`). Everything is scheduled on the arcade's AudioContext and judged in AUDIBLE time (`AudioClock`), minus the device's timing check (`calib[clap|tap].ms`, default `defaultLag`).
- JUDGING (levels.js `RD_RULES`, one commented block): each attack goes to the nearest unjudged note within `lateMs` 360: PERFECT ±70 · GOOD ±140 · OK ±220 · else EARLY/LATE; a note with none = MISS; an attack near no note (in a rest, or one too many) = EXTRA (counted like one more missed note). Attacks before the first note's window (the count-in) never count. Accuracy = the average `value`; a round's best attempt counts; stars from the level's average (`oneStar` 60 / `twoStar` 80 / `threeStar` 95 %); SLOW (−20 %) = no stars; Counting HIDE from the start = +10 % score. THE FEEDBACK: the counting shown again with every group colored (`--rd-ok` on time, `--rd-near` early/late, `--rd-bad` missed/extra; an extra in a rest = the rest red, in a held note = its small syllables red), ONE tip ("Shh! Beat 3 is a rest…", "You missed the & of 2…", "You rushed beat 3!", "You dragged…"), TRY AGAIN / NEXT; `rd-perfect` (≥ 95 %) or `rd-miss` (< 60 %) after the microphone stops.
- `levels.js` = `RD_LEVELS` (11: First Steps, Long Tones, Three and Two (3/4 + 2/4), Eighth Pairs, Lone Eighths, Dotted Quarters, Off the Beat (syncopation + ties), Sixteenth Storm, Triplet Temple, Six-Eight Swing (6/8), Master's Scroll (ties across bar lines, everything, 2–4 measures): time, tempo, `measures` [first, last round], `cells` (ONE-MEASURE rhythms in the text format; a round joins random cells, `tieAcross` ties measures (only between plain notes an eighth or longer), never the same rhythm twice in a level), `rounds` 8; saved by level number: never reorder) + `RD_RULES` + `RD_MARATHON`. The COUNTING setting: SHOW | AUTO (rounds 1–3 shown, 4–6 faded, 7–8 hidden: `RD_RULES.fade`) | HIDE; also in the shared Settings panel.
- DOJO MARATHON (the Endless card): random rhythms from a growing pool of levels (`poolEvery`), 1 → 4 measures, ties across from round 14, the tempo creeping from 80 to 120; study `studyMs`, then an automatic count-in; a rhythm under `loseBelow` 70 % = a life (3); a perfect one = double points. Top 5 per mode (`instKey` 'clap' | 'tap' | 'snare', `setKey` 'marathon'); in `Leaderboard.ENDLESS_GAMES`.
- THE TIMING CHECK (the level select's button; the first performance in a mode opens it): 4 clicks to listen to, then clap/tap on 8 (`Calibration.analyse`, `Sfx.outputRaw` clicks); "I heard the click, not your claps" when the round is the click itself. HEADPHONES (CLAP/SNARE): the headphones check plays `headCheckClicks` clicks while listening; heard `headCheckMax` or more = "It sounds like your speakers are on…" and it stays off.
- PAUSE (the shared kit): pausing stops the clicks and the attempt; RESUME goes back to the rhythm (press PERFORM again; the marathon counts in again by itself).
- Look: THE BAMBOO DOJO = JADE GREEN + GOLD everywhere (never pink, purple or cyan): games.js `color: 'green'`, cabinet `trim: 'green'`, `trim2: 'yellow'` (the gold lettering on its marquee: marquees.js draws a `taiko` title in trim2; the 3D drum's rim is trim2), the art's jade = `--rd-jade`/`-hi`/`-ink` (`--glow-rd-jade`), a teal jade kept apart from the results' green (`--rd-ok`, `--green`), `--rd-night` behind it, a warm-wood drum (`--rd-body`), gold rims and studs; the page's toggles, outlined buttons, panels (jade frame, gold titles), the sheet's frame, the pad (gold rim, jade glow) and the sounding syllable (`--rd-lit` = the jade ink, 5.1:1 on the screen) follow it; tests/rhythm-dojo.spec.js checks the art (no pink/purple/cyan, no flashes). The Sensei (`senseiSVG`, his belt = the level's belt: levels 1–10 = the Band Ninja belts, 11 = Diamond), THE TAIKO (an original neon drum on a stand, `--rd-*` tokens; it bounces on every clap/tap), belt-colored headbands on the level cards and the HUD. Cabinet `shape: 'taiko'` (a drum-barrel top with studs, crossed drumsticks, a little drum on the kick plate), `screen: 'taiko'` (a canvas screen, 2D and 3D: a rhythm with its counting, a playhead, a pulsing drum), marquee scene `taiko` (jade bamboo stalks behind two wooden drums whose gold rims swell in turn on the beat, jade notes drifting up; GN Display, gold), 3D profile `taiko` with a `taiko` topper, menu background scene `taiko` (the drum hall). Avatar reward: the Drum Hall background (a star on Master's Scroll).
- Sounds (screen `rhythm-dojo`, all unpitched): `rhythm-dojo-menu`, `rd-click`, `rd-woodblock` (both scheduled on the audio clock via `Sfx.buffer`, generated until uploaded), `rd-count-in`, `rd-perfect`, `rd-miss`, `rd-level-clear`. The COUNTING BOARD (`rhythm-dojo/counting.html`, linked from the Sound Board, not for students).
- Its judge and timing check live in shared/ (`rhythm-judge.js`, `timing-check.js`, `Calibration.lag/store`), shared with Showtime Malfunction's snare; its `RD_LEVELS` cells are also the snare's rhythm vocabulary (`SNARE_RHYTHMS`): never rename or remove a cell without checking Showtime.
- `?demo`: every level open; CLAP/SNARE: Space = a clap; TAP: Space or the pad. `Arcade.RhythmDojo.state()` / `autoPlay(offsetMs | [per note], {skip, extra: [s], persist})` / `setRound(text, time, tempo)` / `timeline()` / `calClicks()` / `set(patch)` for tests (tests/rhythm-dojo.spec.js).

## Scale Trainer (`scale-trainer/`)
- RENAMED: this game was Scale Audition (id `scale-audition`); it is Scale Trainer, id `scale-trainer` (folder, GAME_ID, progress keys, `gameData`, `menuMusic` `scale-trainer-menu`, `Arcade.ScaleTrainer`). The AUDITION mode, the "Audition Room" level and avatar background, the `AUDITION` table, the `audition` cabinet/scene/marquee/bg ids and the `audition-count-*` / `sa-adjudicator` sounds keep their names: they describe the audition, not the game. The one-time migration `scale-trainer-rename` in storage.js `migrate()` (`renameGame(old, new, {gameData})`) moves every `scale-audition` / `scale-audition:…` progress key, `gameData('scale-audition')` (ALL-STATE READY, best clean runs, chromatic best times, options), the level select's memory, the lobby's CONTINUE, the activity log and queued leaderboard events to the new id, keeping the better value on a clash; `importAll` runs it too, so an old Backup Code restores into the new keys. `scale-audition/index.html` is ONLY a redirect (noindex, no game code, nothing links to it) to `../scale-trainer/index.html` with the query string and hash. A menu track uploaded under the old name (`scale-audition-menu.m4a`) still plays: sounds.js `legacy` → the `scale-trainer-menu-oldfile` entry, tried by `Sfx.gameMenuMusic` after the game's own file. tests/scale-trainer.spec.js checks the migration, the old backup, the redirect and that no other file mentions the old name.
- The GMEA All-State (First Round) / District Honor Band scale audition (Technique Lab; the two auditions have identical requirements, and the first screen says so: "GMEA All-State (First Round) & District Honor Band: same scales, same rules."), in THREE SECTIONS. LISTENS (pitch.js + mic-gate.js); `byMember: true` (each member's GMEA scales differ). Snare: refused (no `unpitched`; `fit` excludes only snare). THE UI KIT: level select (the section cards, a mode row + the cards; `LevelSelect.show` with `gameId` `scale-trainer:<mode>` (Middle School, as always) or `scale-trainer:<section>:<mode>`), pause (the clock stops), results `actsFirst` with THE JUDGE'S SCORE SHEET as the extra.
- THE THREE SECTIONS (`Scales.SECTIONS`, `{id, name, grades, memory}`): MIDDLE SCHOOL `ms` (6th–8th grade · 4 scales · memory: everything below as it always was), CONCERT BAND `cb` (9th–10th grade · 8 scales · music allowed), SYMPHONIC BAND `sb` (11th–12th grade · all 12 scales · music allowed). THE SECTION PICKER = three big cards at the top of the level screen (`#secPick`, `.sa-sec` with `aria-pressed`; each: name, the grade line, its stars `n/12 ★` for this instrument, its ALL-STATE READY badge, and "GMEA All-State (First Round) & District Honor Band"); remembered in `gameData('scale-trainer').section` (default `ms`). Inside a section everything works the same (PRACTICE, AUDITION levels, CHROMATIC CHALLENGE, score sheet, fingering cards), built from that section's data (game.js `secData` = `SEC`: scales, chrom, sheet time, tempo, progress key, levels, the mic range).
- THE AUDITION TABLE = `AUDITION[section][member]` in `shared/scales.js` (next to `START`, which is unchanged; a commented header names the source sheets): `{time (the sheet's seconds), scales: [[concert key, the WRITTEN starting note, octaves 1 | 2 | 3], …] in the order printed, tempo? (the sheet's ♩ =)}` or `{time, sameAs: '<member>'}` (barisax = altosax, Baritone T.C. = trumpet, Euphonium B.C. = trombone in cb/sb). THE GMEA SHEETS ARE THE SOURCE: EDIT AUDITION, NOT THE GAME. The written start note also fixes the written key's spelling (`majorScale(midi, spelling)`): trumpet concert B = written D♭ major (5 flats), horn concert B = G♭ major, clarinet concert E = F♯ major. `Scales.AUDITION_ORDER[section]` = ms F, B♭, E♭, A♭; cb G, C, F, B♭, E♭, A♭, D♭, G♭; sb G, C, F, B♭, E♭, A♭, D♭, G♭, B, E, A, D ("must be performed in the order printed"; not `Scales.LIST`'s order). `Scales.CONCERT` = the 12 keys' names + pitch classes. MS times: clarinet and bassoon 75, bells 75 (the 2025-26 GMEA mallet sheet; its F scale from F3), the rest 60. Helpers (every reader takes a section id, default 'ms'): `auditionEntry(m, sec)` (sameAs resolved), `audition(member, key, sec)`, `auditionScales(m, sec)` (in order), `auditionChromatic(m, sec)`, `auditionTime(m, sec)`, `auditionTempo(m, sec)` (cb ♩ = 132 except the horn / tenor / oboe sheets, which print none; sb only horn ♩ = 144), `auditionRange(m, sec)` (the widest of its scales and its chromatic, written midi). `audition` = the scale up and down + its arpeggio for ANY NUMBER OF OCTAVES (1 octave: 21 notes, 3-5-8-5-3-1; 2 octaves: 41, 3-5-8-10-12-15-12-10-8-5-3-1; 3 octaves (flute C, clarinet G/F/F♯/E, bassoon B♭): 61, 3-5-8-10-12-15-17-19-22 and back), spelled by scales.js (`majorScale`, `keySignature`, `shown`: never a second speller), each note with `beats` + `measure` = the sheet's 4/4 rhythm picture (`rhythmOf(octaves)` = 2n scale bars + n arpeggio bars + the whole note; only a picture, never judged): the SAME note values for every octave count, more octaves = more measures: scale bars = quarter + 2 eighths + 4 eighths (7 notes), arpeggio bars = quarter, 2 eighths, quarter, 2 eighths (6 notes; the first starts on the scale's last note), a final whole note. 1 octave = 4 bars `| 1–7 | 8–2 | 1 3 5 8 5 3 | 1 |`; 2 octaves = 7 bars `| 1–7 | 8–14 | 15–9 | 8–2 | 1 3 5 8 10 12 | 15 12 10 8 5 3 | 1 |`; 3 octaves = 10 bars. Never sixteenths: the staff wraps to more lines instead. ARTICULATION as on the sheets: tongue going up, slur coming down: `slurs` = [first, last] note indexes, the scale's top note down to the tonic where the scale ends (n octaves: 7n→14n) and the arpeggio's top note down to the final whole note (17n→the last); the arpeggio going up stays tongued; mallets (`family: 'percussion'`) have none (mallets can't slur); the chromatic none (tongued or slurred is the student's choice). THE CHROMATIC: Middle School = `Arcade.chromaticScale` over the member's GMEA range (instruments.js `chromatic` STAYS THE MIDDLE SCHOOL RANGE; sharps up, flats down; `AUDITION_CHROM` = the MS-only mallet range F4–F6, the bells member is unchanged; the GMEA mallet sheet says from C: Mat: decide); high school (cb and sb share one sheet set) = `Scales.CHROMATIC_HS[member]` = [lowest, highest] WRITTEN (flute C4–C7, oboe and saxes B♭3–F6, clarinet E3–G6, bass clarinet E3–C6, horn F3–A5, trumpet / Baritone T.C. F♯3–C6, bassoon B♭1–B♭4, trombone / Euphonium B.C. E2–B♭4, tuba E1–B♭3, bells C4–C6 (the mallet sheet: "2-octave chromatic starting on C, after the major scales, not in the time limit")): sharps going up, flats coming down, the lowest note too (A♯3 up, B♭3 down; F♯3 up, G♭3 down), the top note played once. THE SECTION'S RANGE: the mic range (`Pitch.setRange`, Middle School: the member's GMEA range as always; cb / sb: `auditionRange`, sounding), the staff and the fingering card follow the section. Mallets: 2 octaves each; no range check (they audition on a bigger instrument than the bell kit): `Pitch.setRange(MALLET_RANGE)` (a wide sounding range, never the bells range), judged by pitch class. MAT: CONFIRM (in the table's comments): SB tenor sax 2:15 (the sheet's footer), the CB trombone / tuba times (2:00 assumed: no time printed on the older sheets), the CB oboe sheet's year (2024-25: not in the 2025-26 folder), the bells' MS chromatic (C vs F).
- ARTICULATION (winds): the sheet draws each slur as a curve above the notes (it follows the line of the notes and bends just enough to clear every note and stem, `sa-slur`, split at a line break: the part on each row runs to the row's edge, `data-part` start / middle / end / whole); "Tongue going up, slur coming down." (`#artLine`) under the scale's name in Practice and on levels 1–2 (not mallets, not the chromatic). FEEDBACK from `Pitch.onAttack` (every attack's time, `G.attacks`): a note the follower COUNTED (only those: never a mistake on a note that wasn't counted) had an attack if one falls between the previous note's count and its own, both shifted back by `ATTACK_LAG_MS`; tongued notes (not under a slur) want one, notes under a slur after its first want none (the first may have one: not judged); ✓ when at least `ARTICULATION_TOLERANCE` (80 %) of each kind matches. The score sheet has one line per scale: "Articulation: ✓" or "Articulation: check tonguing going up / slurring coming down" (whichever went wrong). `ARTICULATION_COUNTS = false`: attack detection at speed isn't reliable enough on every mic to cost a student stars, so it never changes stars, clean notes or the badge (true = 3 ★ also need every scale's ✓). ?demo Space tongues where the music says (an attack going up, none under a slur).
- HEARING (game.js, every number in the TUNING block at its top): the expected notes are followed IN ORDER from `Pitch.onFrame` (never `onHeld`: students play up to ~2 notes a second and may slur): a new note = the same pitch class for `STABLE_FRAMES` (3) frames AND a change from the last counted pitch, or a fresh `Pitch.onAttack` (a repeated note), or `GAP_FRAMES` of silence first. Winds: `Pitch.setRange(member.soundLow, soundHigh)`; judged by pitch class (the scale's order catches octave mistakes). Right = green (`ok`), advance; wrong = the expected note red (`bad`) once, still waited for, then amber (`fix`, not clean) when played; the NEXT expected note instead of this one = this one red ("skipped"), advance two; the note just counted, held or played again = ignored. No sound per note; the clock pauses during `Pitch.isSuppressed`, the pause and a hidden tab.
- MODES (`gameData('scale-trainer').mode`): PRACTICE (a card per scale in audition order + Chromatic, labeled like `Scales.build`'s label; no clock, no stars; LOOP (a running count of clean runs), SLOW GUIDE ♩ = 60/80/100 + the sheet's own tempo where it prints one ("♩ = 132, audition tempo" in Concert Band, 144 for the Symphonic Band horn: `auditionTempo`, a `data-sheet` button added per section) (a silent moving highlight along the rhythm picture; never judges; no metronome: a click would mute the mic), NOTE BY NOTE (one note big + its name + the fingering from `shared/fingerings.js` via `Arcade.Masher` when the member has one); "Best clean run" per scale = `gameData.practice[member][scale id]` (tenths; cb / sb: `practice.cb[member]…`)). AUDITION (the levels): the adjudicator's line ("Please play your scales in order, from memory."; optional voice `sa-adjudicator`) + a CLASSIC countdown (`voicePrefix 'audition'`, falls back to Dojo Duel's voice) while the mic waits, then ONE clock for every scale of the section, the next staff when a scale's last note is heard, no restarts. Levels (`LEVELS` in game.js): LEVELS 1–2 NEVER ASK FOR MEMORY OR TIME: the full staff (notes, key signature, slurs) for EVERY scale, the adjudicator says "Please play your scales in order." (no "from memory"), NO TIMER (no time bar, no amber, no "Time. Thank you.": the run ends when the last scale's last note is heard; a stopwatch that pauses with suppression and PAUSE; the score sheet shows "Your time 1:48 · Audition limit 1:00" as information only, never ✗): Warm-Up Room ("Music shown · No timer"; the current note's name under its notehead, `.sa-name`) · Practice Room ("Music shown · No timer"; the staff only); LEVELS 3–4 = (MIDDLE SCHOOL) from memory (only "Concert F (your G Major)"; "Please play your scales in order, from memory.") and TIMED: Hallway ("Memory · Timed", the sheet time × `mult` 1.25: the first timed level) · Audition Room ("Memory · Audition time", × 1.0 = the sheet time). TIME (levels 3–4): a time bar, amber in the last `LOW_TIME_S` 15 s, NO alarm; at 0 the scale being played may finish (the GMEA rule), then a calm "Time. Thank you." (`TIME_UP_IDLE_S` 12 s with nothing heard ends it too); scales not started score 0. Stars: 1 = every scale finished (in time on levels 3–4), 2 = + ≥ 90 % of the notes clean, 3 = every note clean. 3 ★ on level 4 = ALL-STATE READY, per section (`gameData.ready`: Middle School `ready[member]` = the date, where it always was; Concert Band `ready.cb[member]`, Symphonic Band `ready.sb[member]`; member ids never clash with cb / sb; game.js `readSec` reads without adding anything, `bySec` writes): a badge on the results, the level screen and its section card. MIDDLE SCHOOL's unlocks the avatar background Audition Room (`auditionroom`, scene `audition`; rule `{game: 'scale-trainer', level: 4, stars: 3, exact: true}`: `exact` = the plain 'scale-trainer' key only, `store.bestLevelStars(game, lvl, suffix, exact)`); CONCERT BAND's the "Concert Band Ready" name plate (plate `cbready`, rule `suffix: ':cb'`), SYMPHONIC BAND's the "Symphonic Band Ready" plate (`sbready`, `suffix: ':sb'`) (`.av-plate-cbready/-sbready` in theme.css, avatar-code.js TABLE). THE HIGH SCHOOL MEMORY RULE: the HS sheets say "Scales do not have to be memorized, but must be performed in the order printed", so Concert Band and Symphonic Band levels 3–4 KEEP THE MUSIC SHOWN (`LEVELS_HS`: "Music shown · Timed", "Music shown · Audition time"; "Please play your scales in order."); the optional FROM MEMORY switch (`#memSw` in `#auditionOpts`, cb / sb audition mode only, `gameData.fromMemory`) hides the staff on levels 3–4 (`memoryOk`; the cards say "From memory · Timed"; the adjudicator adds "from memory"): such a run adds "★ From memory" to its score sheet (`#memMark`), its best line and its level card (`gameData.memRuns.<cb|sb>[member][level]`); the same stars either way. Middle School is unchanged (levels 3–4 always from memory, no switch). Time: levels 1–2 no timer, level 3 = the section's sheet time × 1.25, level 4 = the sheet time. PROGRESS: Middle School under the plain key as always (`setLevel('scale-trainer', member id, level 1–4, {stars, best: % clean})`, no migration), Concert Band `scale-trainer:cb`, Symphonic Band `scale-trainer:sb` (same shape); `maxStars` 36 = 3 sections × 12 (`allStars` counts every `scale-trainer:…` key, so the floor, the leaderboard and the Prize Counter's star sources count them normally). CHROMATIC CHALLENGE: the section's chromatic up and down, a stopwatch, no limit; "Tongued or slurred, any rhythm you choose — the judges listen for evenness, accuracy and speed."; stars (1 finished, 2 = ≥ 2.5 clean notes a second + ≤ 2 misses, 3 = ≥ 4 a second + no misses: `CHROM_STARS`) and the best time in `gameData.chrom[member]` (cb / sb: `chrom.cb[member]`, `chrom.sb[member]`) (outside `games`, so they never count in `maxStars`).
- THE FINGERING CARD (game.js `Card`, `CARD` numbers; `.fc*` in style.css, tokens `--sa-tap`, `--sa-bar*`): tap ANY NOTE where a staff shows (Practice with every option, levels 1–2, the Chromatic Challenge; NOTE BY NOTE's note and the live dots under it) or a DOT on the results' score sheet (levels 3–4 hide the staff while playing: there only the score sheet): a small card next to the note with the written name in the scale's spelling ("F♯4" going up, "G♭4" coming down), the concert pitch small, the PRIMARY fingering drawn with shared/diagrams.js (Button Masher's Chart drawing, display only: no pressable keys) and the alternates in words like the Chart ("Also: 3"); trombone: its position ("4th position", alternates "or 6th"; the euphonium B.C. shows its valves); MALLETS: no fingering, a small SVG of the bars (`barsSVG`: about two octaves centered on the note, naturals below, accidentals raised, shorter as the pitch rises, like Chime Heist's kit; no other game's keyboard drawing is shared code) with that bar lit + "Mallets: the lit bar". A TAP picks the NEAREST note in the row it lands on, in a band at least 44 px tall (`CARD.hit`; the staff's notes are as wide as their spacing), on pointerdown with preventDefault + `Arcade.holdGuard` + `touch-action: manipulation`, so taps never move the page. The tapped note gets a ring (`.fc-ring`, a dot `.fc-on`); another note moves the card; the same note, a tap outside, Esc (window capture: before the pause menu) or ✕ closes it. KEYS: every staff note (`g.fc-note`) and score-sheet dot is focusable, one tab stop per staff / dot row (roving tabindex), Enter/Space opens (never the ?demo Space), ←/→ (↑/↓, Home/End) move with the card following. PLACEMENT (`place`): never over the tapped note or the current (blue) note, on screen: below/above the note's whole row first (its other notes stay tappable), else below/above/right/left of the note; redone on every judged note (`drawNow`), scroll and resize. IT NEVER GETS IN THE WAY: the microphone keeps listening, nothing pauses, restarts or is judged, NO SOUND (it would mute the mic); pausing closes it; NOTE BY NOTE keeps its own fingering, a tapped dot's card shows on top without changing the note it waits for. `Arcade.ScaleTrainer.card()` / `openCard(kind, i, run)` / `closeCard()` for tests.
- THE SCORE SHEET (the results' extra): per scale its name, ✓ finished / ✓ finished after time / ✗ time, clean notes out of the total, a row of note dots (green clean, amber fixed, red missed/skipped, dim not played) and the time used; then the total time against the limit.
- THE STAFF = `Arcade.staffSVG` rows (the student's clef + key signature; a 4/4 time signature, beams, bar lines and the final bar through `staffSVG`'s `extra` + `noteGlyph`'s `whole`/`stemUp`/`stemTo` (ui.js)); measures packed into rows as wide as the screen allows (never under `MIN_SCALE`), redrawn on resize; the current note blue (`cur`), the guide ringed (`guide`).
- Look: THE AUDITION ROOM (`--sa-*` tokens: wall, wood, the judges' blue cloth, lamps, a music stand). Menu background scene `audition` (bg-scenes.js: the music stand at the left, the judges' table with two lamps at the right, the lamps breathing over 6 s, the middle dark). Cabinet `shape: 'audition'` (a music-stand top, a stand painted on the lower left, the judge's clipboard with ticks on the right), `screen: 'audition'` (a canvas screen: a scale turning green note by note, a time bar, "ALL-STATE READY!"), marquee scene `audition` (a music stand at each end, a scale's notes lighting one at a time up and down, a smooth swell and fade), 3D profile `audition` with a `stand` topper (a music stand holding a lit sheet). Sounds (screen `scale-trainer`): `scale-trainer-menu`, `sa-adjudicator` (optional voice), `audition-count-3/-2/-1/-go`; nothing during a scale.
- `?demo`: every level open; tap Space = the next note (hold = keep playing), W = a wrong note, K = skip one; `Pitch.demoNote` drives the real follower. `Arcade.ScaleTrainer.state()` (+ `section`, `fromMemory`, `guide`) / `want()` / `heard(pc)` / `attack(t)` / `articulation()` / `scales()` / `chromatic()` (the current section's) / `section()` / `setSection(id)` / `range()` / `guideTempos()` / `sheetTime()` / `setMs(ms)` / `begin(kind, arg)` / `ARTICULATION_COUNTS` for tests: tests/scale-trainer.spec.js (the three sections' tables, 1/2/3-octave shapes, spelling from the start note, the order, sameAs, a save from before the sections, the cb / sb keys and plates, the memory rule + FROM MEMORY, the chromatic per section, the HS fingering coverage, bells MS 75 s / F3).

## Lost Signal (`lost-signal/`)
- THE UI KIT: intro = `UI.intro` (theme `ls-theme`); the kit pause stops the tones (the transmission is marked interrupted and plays again, free, on RESUME), holds the echo slot clock (`echo.slotStart` restored) and keeps the game's own mic turn-taking (re-applied after RESUME); `Arcade.LostSignal.paused()`.
- Pitch-memory game (deep-space radio): the game plays a short melody (a transmission) with `Arcade.tones`, the student echoes it in order. Its pattern generator and echo flow are SHARED with Vanishing Ink (`shared/patterns.js`, `shared/echo.js`: PATTERNS / ECHO above); Lost Signal keeps only its weights (`SIGNAL_GEN`), the tones, FIND THE SIGNAL, replays and the scope. LISTENS (pitch.js + mic-gate.js + sfx.js + tones.js + endless.js). NOTES × ORDER picks the note set (`ModePicker.sequence(...).pool`, one item per sounding pitch); Scale Order = stepwise transmissions only. Group-keyed progress (`picker.state.progressKey`, like Ghost Notes), `maxStars` 24. Snare: not allowed (needs pitch); bells play it.
- THE MIC TAKES TURNS (the Hard-rules exception): `mic(false)` = `Pitch.pauseListening(true)` + `Sfx.sync()` before every tone and every mic:false sound; `mic(true)` only after `tones done + RULES.listenAfterMs` and `lost-signal-your-turn` (+ 250 ms), with `ignoreCurrent()`. Menu music (`lost-signal-music`, built-in chiptune until uploaded) only on the level screens; `setMusic(null)` for play.
- Patterns: `levels.js` = `SIGNAL_LEVELS` (8: count, len, pool, leap (half steps: 2 step, 4 3rd, 7 5th, 12 octave), repeats, label, find, replays, noteMs, gapMs, story, nowWhat), `SIGNAL_RULES` (slotMs 6 s, listenAfterMs 400, replayCost, findCost, findReveal, star rates, `register` [55, 79] = concert G3–G5), `SIGNAL_GEN` (move weights, recover after a skip, no two big skips in a row), `SIGNAL_ENDLESS`, `SIGNAL_ENDING`. Generated in SOUNDING pitch, played shifted by whole octaves as a block (`shiftFor`) into the register; answers match by pitch class (any octave). Never the same transmission twice in a row.
- Input: `Pitch.onAttack` (with its pc) fills the next slot; `onHeld` too when no attack was heard for that note within 700 ms (soft entries), so a long note fills ONE slot and a repeated note needs a new tongue. Slots fill right or wrong and move on; `slotMs` without a note = missed. FIND THE SIGNAL (levels 5–6): try notes until one matches the hidden first note (`findCost` each; "Show me" after `findReveal`), then echo the whole pattern. REPLAY SIGNAL before the first note (`replays` per transmission, −`replayCost` each); "Hear it again" on the result (free). Result: `staffSVG` in written pitch: gold right, coral wrong + "you played D", coral missed in a dotted box. Stars from accuracy (70 / 85 / 100 % + no replays). The Source cleared = `SIGNAL_ENDING`. First visit: SIGNAL CHECK (`gameData('lost-signal').signalChecked`).
- DEEP SPACE SCAN (the ENDLESS card, shared/endless.js Top 5 per member + note set): the same pattern + one note per perfect round; a missed round costs a heart and replays the same pattern; labeled for 3 rounds; faster every 5 rounds (`minNoteMs`); score = 100 per right note + 50 × length for a perfect round; GAME OVER = longest signal, score, rounds survived.
- Look: `--ls-*` tokens; the scope canvas (radar sweep + a ping per note + waveform; still under reduced motion; dpr ≤ 1.5, rAF only while something moves); static on the first-note box = slow low-contrast noise (2.5 steps/s). Cabinet: `shape: 'signal'`, `screen: 'signal'`, marquee scene `radio` (font GN Neon), 3D profile `signal` with a `dish` topper. `?demo`: Space = the note the game wants (`Pitch.demoTarget`), W = a wrong one, 1–5 = held first-five notes. `A.LostSignal.state()` for tests.

## Vanishing Ink (`vanishing-ink/`)
- THE UI KIT: intro = `UI.intro` (theme `vi-theme`, the Ink Master as hero); the kit pause holds the study clock, the fade, the brush-in and the slot clock; `Arcade.VanishingInk.paused()`.
- Reading-memory game (Band Ninja world, the step after Ghost Notes): a short line of notes is brushed onto the Ink Master's parchment scroll, the ink FADES or VANISHES, the student plays the notes back in order from memory. LISTENS (pitch.js + mic-gate.js + sfx.js + patterns.js + echo.js + endless.js). NOTES × ORDER picks the note set (`A.patterns.noteSet`); patterns from `A.patterns.generate` with `INK_GEN`; Scale Order = stepwise only. Group-keyed progress (`picker.state.progressKey`, like Ghost Notes), `maxStars` 24, zones note-reading + ninja-dojo. Snare: not allowed. Answers match by concert pitch class (any octave).
- A ROUND (`nextRound`): `mic(true)` at once (listening, `ignoreCurrent`), phase `brush` (each note appears `RULES.brushMs` apart, WAAPI, `ink-brush` each; all at once under reduced motion) → `study` (the rAF game clock `R.t`, `studyMs`; the ink-drop timer; FADE: `setInk` lowers every note's opacity together over the last `fade` share, `ink-fade` once; the study clock never pauses for sounds) → `inkGone()` (VANISH: `puff()` + `ink-vanish`; `Pitch.ignoreCurrent()`, phase `echo`, `echo.start()`, `ink-your-turn`) → the ECHO FLOW (`slotMs` 6 s each, `pauseSuppressed`) → `finishRound()` (`mic(false)`, `Echo.drawResult` on the scroll: the ink comes back marked, "4 of 5 notes remembered. +pts"). While the ink shows (phases brush/study/reveal) NOTHING counts: any attack, held note or `onFrame` reading shows "READ, DON'T PLAY YET" (`#nope`, `R.warned`). The scroll's staff is `Echo.drawResult(… plain: true, idPrefix: 'ink')` so the results land in the same place; notes are `.ink-note` groups drawn in `--vi-ink`.
- REVEAL SCROLL: before the first note only (`echo.i === 0`), `L.reveals` per ROUND, `RULES.revealMs` of ink, −`revealCost` of that round's points; the slot clock restarts after it. Stars: `oneStar` 70 %, `twoStar` 85 %, 3 = 100 % and no reveals in the level. A star opens the next level; Invisible Master cleared = `INK_ENDING` (the Ink Master's last words).
- `levels.js` = `INK_LEVELS` (8: name, rounds, len, pool, leap (2 steps, 4 thirds, 5 mixed up to a 4th, 7 fifths, 12 all), repeats, study s, ink 'fade'|'vanish', fade share, reveals, say (the Ink Master's intro line), nowWhat), `INK_ENDING`, `INK_RULES`, `INK_GEN`, `INK_ENDLESS`.
- ENDLESS SCROLL (the ENDLESS card, shared/endless.js Top 5 per member + note set): starts with `startLen` 2, a perfect round adds one note to the SAME pattern (`A.patterns.next`); study = max(`minStudy` + `minPerNote` × n, (`study` + `perNote` × n) × `shrink`^(round − 1)); FADE for `fadeRounds` 5, then VANISH; 3 lives (any wrong/missed note costs one and the same scroll returns); no reveals; score 100 per right note + 50 × length for a perfect round; GAME OVER = longest scroll, score, rounds survived (`ink-game-over`, `ink-high-score`).
- THE INK MASTER (`inkMasterSVG(mood 'calm'|'happy')` in game.js, `.im-*` in style.css, `--vi-master*` tokens): an original friendly old octopus calligrapher in a black-belt headband, white brows and mustache, a brush in one arm. Keep him kind and original (no franchise ninjas).
- Sounds (screen `vanishing-ink`, every one unpitched noise, `gen` fallbacks): `vanishing-ink-music` (loop, FILE ONLY: no built-in chiptune; menus only), during play (mic: true, short): `ink-brush`, `ink-fade`, `ink-vanish`, `ink-your-turn`, `ink-note-correct`, `ink-note-wrong`, `ink-reveal-scroll`; mic: false: `ink-round-complete`, `ink-level-clear`, `ink-life-lost`, `ink-game-over`, `ink-high-score` (level results also use the shared `level-failed`, `star-earned`, `new-high-score`).
- Look: `--vi-*` tokens (parchment, rods, ink, seal, the Ink Master), the Dojo Duel night (`--dd-*` lanterns + floor), titles in `GN Brush`. The staff is large (up to 40vh; sized for each scroll's own notes, results in the same size), dark ink on light parchment, thicker stems/lines. Reduced motion: no brush-in animation, no puffs, no lantern sway (the fade itself stays: it's the game). Cabinet: `shape: 'ink'` (scroll rods beside the marquee, an ink splash, a fading note, a brush), `screen: 'ink'`, marquee scene `ink` (font GN Brush), 3D profile `ink` with a `scroll` topper. `?demo`: Space = the note the game wants (during study: the first note, to test the warning), W = a wrong one, 1–5 = held first-five notes. `A.VanishingInk.state()` / `.echo()` for tests.

## Dojo Duel (`dojo-duel/`)
- THE UI KIT: the pause is the kit's, placed in the center strip `#midC` (`place`: both players reach it in Tabletop; RESUME · RESTART · SETTINGS · CHANGE SETUP · BACK TO ARCADE GAMES (the kit's, `onArcade` stops the loop and the dojo sounds; it replaced the old EXIT)); results = `A.UI.results.render` inside each half (REMATCH · CHANGE SETUP · EXIT). The text below describes the match itself.
- Two-player note-reading race on ONE device (Band Ninja world). NO microphone (never load `pitch.js`/`mic-gate.js`); `player: 'all'` + `players: 2` in games.js (START goes straight to the game, no instrument needed), `maxStars: 0`, no effect on Note Ninja progress; the floor shows `summary` (the dojo record).
- SETUP (remembered in `gameData('dojo-duel').setup`): mode 2 PLAYERS | SOLO VS. SENSEI (Easy/Medium/Hard = `DUEL_SENSEI`: react s ± spread, wrong-tap chance); match length 7/10/15 (`DUEL_RULES.lengths`); layout TABLETOP (default on touch screens) | SIDE BY SIDE. Each player: avatar (Player 1 = the device's avatar, Player 2 = the GUEST avatar: EDIT opens `AvatarCreator` with `guest`, SURPRISE ME re-rolls it), name ("Player 1"/"Player 2" by default, or the avatar's safe name), clef (treble/bass; no alto: no instrument reads it), NOTES (First 5 / the four scales / Chromatic, labeled by their written key), and BELT = any Note Ninja belt with a star on this device (`bestLevelStars('note-ninja', …)`, any instrument) or the next one; all in `?demo`. Belts are the handicap: each player's notes = `buildSequence` with THEIR belt's `pool` (White = the smaller pool), `guides` (`staffGuides`) and `relabel` from `note-ninja/levels.js` `NINJA_BELTS` (loaded, never copied). Notes are written for the saved instrument if it reads that clef, else trumpet (treble) / trombone (bass). The Sensei reads Player 1's kind of notes. A key map shows the keyboard groups.
- A POINT (`M.phase` 'count' → 'play' → 'result'; every timing in `DUEL_PACING` at the top of levels.js): COUNTDOWN (`countdown()`; its beats and sounds come from `shared/countdown.js`, voicePrefix 'dojo', on the game clock): pads locked + `.waiting` (dimmed a little), empty staffs, 3-2-1 in each half's `.hf-call` (so each reads upright for its player in Tabletop), scale-in only; SOUNDS: CLASSIC = the spoken `dojo-count-3`/`-2`/`-1` on each number, QUICK = the `dojo-count` tick, OFF = none. Style = setup `S.count` 'quick' (`quickStep` 350 ms) | 'classic' (`classicStep` 1 s) | 'off' (`offMs` 0.4 s gap, no numbers), remembered in `setup`; MATCH START (and REMATCH): once `Sfx.whenReady(COUNT_SOUNDS, 800)` (the setup screen `Sfx.preloadScreen('dojo-duel')`s every dojo-*/sensei-* sound) and no voice is still speaking, the gong `dojo-begin` + the Sensei's `sensei-begin` + "Begin!" together, then the first note's CLASSIC 3-2-1 `introGapMs` 0.4 s after the longer ends (≤ `introMaxMs` 3 s, never while the Sensei speaks; `M.introMs`); the first note of a match is always CLASSIC, so voices never overlap, and so is the note after a point that leaves someone at match point (`M.tense`). Then `showNote()` draws both notes and unlocks both pads in ONE call inside the game-clock rAF tick (same frame), `pt.t0` = now (the Sensei's reaction starts here), `dojo-count-go` ("Go!", every style; falls back to `dojo-reveal`, never both). Answers = `it.n` (letter + acc, as Note Ninja); taps outside 'play' do nothing. First correct tap wins: correct taps are collected for `tieMs` and the smallest `ev.timeStamp` wins (multi-touch pointerdown). A wrong tap = `stunMs` (1 s) dizzy: pad locked + swaying, avatar with orbiting stars; the other player keeps going. Both wrong, or `noteMs` (6 s) with no answer = no point. RESULT MOMENT (`endPoint`, `resultMs` / `noPointMs` 1.5 s, pads locked): both notes gold/coral with the name next to them, the right button `.good.answer` on both pads, a wrong tap stays `.bad` + "You tapped G. It's E."; a point = the winner's half `.won` (belt-color glow), their ninja `.strike` toward the other (`--sx/--sy` per layout), the other `.stagger`, "+1" flies to their score (`flyPoint`, layout positions via `centerIn`, `flyMs`; reduced motion: no flight), "POINT!" + a Sensei line, `dojo-strike` + `dojo-point` (+ `sensei-fast` / `sensei-point`, below); nobody = "Too slow, ninjas!" / a both-wrong line + `dojo-no-point`. MATCH POINT: the `.mpb` banner on that half during the result moment + the center line + that half's tag; the winning point goes straight to the victory screen after its result moment. The clock is a game clock (stops on PAUSE, Esc, a hidden tab). No power-ups.
- LAYOUTS: `.duel` is fixed full screen (`touch-action: none`; gesturestart/dblclick/contextmenu/selectstart and touchmove prevented; nothing scrolls or zooms). TABLETOP = top/bottom halves, the top half (Player 2) `rotate(180deg)`, the center strip's 2P score rotated too; if the device turns during a match, `holdOrientation()` counter-rotates the whole duel so the halves stay with their players (and the setup note suggests Rotation Lock). SIDE BY SIDE = left/right, both upright. Solo = side by side in landscape, stacked upright in portrait. Results render INSIDE each half (so the top one faces its player): VICTORY!/WELL FOUGHT!, both ninjas bowing, accuracy, average answer time, the dojo record, PRACTICE THESE (up to 4 notes: wrong taps, then no answer, then lost, then slowest, on a mini staff in their clef), REMATCH / CHANGE SETUP / EXIT.
- Keyboards: Player 1 Q W E R T Y U = A–G, A = ♭, S = ♯; Player 2 1–7 = A–G, 8 = ♭, 9 = ♯ (tap the sign first, like the buttons; every belt works). Solo: both groups play for Player 1.
- Saved: `gameData('dojo-duel')` = `{setup, record: {'<player name>': wins}, sensei: {easy|medium|hard: {w, l}}}` (in the Arcade Backup Code with all gameData). The Sensei's lines = `DUEL_LINES` in levels.js (kind to both players).
- Sounds (mic: false, pitched is fine): `dojo-music` (setup), `dojo-match-music` (match), `dojo-begin` (the gong at every match start, with sensei-begin), `dojo-count` (the QUICK tick), `dojo-count-3`/`-2`/`-1` (spoken CLASSIC numbers, fallback `dojo-count`), `dojo-count-go` (the reveal, fallback `dojo-reveal`; ONE FILE PER WORD, trimmed to start at once, numbers < 0.8 s; nothing ever stops a sound: every play is its own source), `dojo-strike` (the winner's strike), `dojo-no-point`, `dojo-point`, `dojo-wrong`, `dojo-match-point`, `dojo-victory`; the Sensei's voice (short generated jingles until recorded): `sensei-begin` (every match start, with the gong, before the first 3-2-1), `sensei-fast` (after a PLAYER's point won in under `DUEL_PACING.fastMs`, any mode), `sensei-point` (ONLY Solo, when the computer Sensei scores; never both with sensei-fast), `sensei-victory` (every match end). NO TWO VOICES AT ONCE, NOTHING LEFT OVER: a point's effects are `Sfx.sequence([...], 80, {channel: 'dojo', maxStep: 900})` (strike, point, match-point) and its Sensei line a separate `'dojo:voice'` sequence (starts with the strike, after any "Go!"; not on the match-winning point: sensei-victory follows); a countdown waits (`voiceWaitMs`) until the voice lane is done and no voice speaks (a match-point countdown also waits for the MATCH POINT sound to start); victory, REMATCH, Setup and Exit call `Sfx.cancelAll('dojo')` (+ `Sfx.hush()` on leaving/rematch).
- Look: `--dd-*` tokens (night, wood, paper lanterns), belt-color headbands on the avatars (`.band`), the neon center line; reduced motion = no bob/jump/dizzy/bow/sway. Cabinet: `shape: 'duel'` (dojo roof, two-player panel, lanterns), `screen: 'duel'`, marquee scene `duel` (lanterns, crossed bamboo, belt ribbons; font GN Neon), 3D profile `duel` (`twoPlayer`, topper `lanterns`). `?demo`: every belt open, the answers under each staff; `Arcade.Duel.state()` (incl. `phase`, `countKind`) / `setup()` / `rotate(angle)` / `tap(pi, letter, timeStamp)` for tests.

## Note Ninja (`note-ninja/`)
- Note identification with NO microphone: never load `pitch.js` or `mic-gate.js` there, never ask for the mic. It loads `sfx.js` and plays the named events.
- NOTES × ORDER like every note-reading game (`Arcade.ModePicker`). The right answer is the note's real name, key signature included (a B in F major is B♭, in Random and Scale Order alike), spelled as shown (C♯ ≠ D♭). Answer pad: ♭ ♮ ♯ act as a Shift for the next letter (♮ default, resets after each letter; shown only when the level's notes have any accidentals), then A–G. Keys A–G and 1/2/3 on Chromebooks. `?demo` shows the answer under the buttons.
- Belts = levels in `note-ninja/levels.js` (`NINJA_BELTS`: name (one of `shared/belts.js`, which supplies the color and sparkle), count, time, guides, onStaff for read-ahead, pool, `relabel: false` = the letter buttons don't show ♭/♯ after the Shift). They are Mat's 10 Band Ninja ranks, in order: White, Yellow, Orange, Green, Blue, Purple, Red, Brown, Black, Diamond (`maxStars` 30). Belt colors are `--belt-*` tokens in theme.css. Progress is saved by belt number, so never reorder or insert belts without a one-time migration in `storage.js` `migrate()` (flagged in `data.migrated`; `ninja-10-belts` moved old 8 → 9 and 7 → 8 when Red was added). Unlocking = the previous belt has a star, or the belt itself already has stars. Scoring and stars are in `NINJA_RULES`. Progress: the PROGRESS KEYS table (`note-ninja`, `note-ninja:scale-<id>`, `note-ninja:random-<id>`, `note-ninja:order-first5`…); the `ninja-10-belts` migration moved every key that is `note-ninja` or starts with `note-ninja:`, so any key saved since is already in 10-belt order.
- ENDLESS (`G.endless`, the ∞ card, `NINJA_ENDLESS` in levels.js): SPEED 1 = the White belt (10 s a note); time per note = time1 ÷ SPEED (never under `minTime`), set when each note starts; at each new group the guides fade, read-ahead grows (`readAhead`) and the button hints stop (`noHintsFrom`); the ninja's belt follows the pace. The run clock only counts while a note waits for an answer. A wrong answer or a timeout = a heart (the note turns red, the run moves on). Notes come in chunks from `ModePicker.sequence` (the smaller pool until `smallPoolUntil`, then the whole set: never a note outside it). `A.Ninja.state()` for tests.
- THE SENSEI BESIDE THE STAFF (game.js `drawGroup` / `pose` / `slice`): the Sensei (`A.senseiSVG`) stands INSIDE the scroll, in a slot at the right end of the staff drawing (`SENSEI_W` units, the drawing's full height, `#nnSensei`): the drawing keeps the width it always had (`[290, 330, 380, 420][onStaff] + key signature`), the staff lines stop before the slot and the notes share the rest, so nothing is drawn smaller and he never covers a note (read-ahead too), the clef, the key signature or the guides. He wears the belt (the level's `L.color`, Endless's pace belt). Poses: 'ready' while you read, 'strike' on a right answer, 'hmm' on a wrong answer or a timeout (`POSE_MS`, then 'ready'). THE SLICE (`slice(k, big)`): a bright streak along a rising cut through the note head, the note (gold) split into two clipped halves that slide apart and fade (`SLICE_MS` 300; a combo milestone = a longer, golden streak, plus the combo pop); the note itself is gone, its gold name stays. It never delays anything: the next note and the timer start exactly when they always did (read-ahead at once, a new group after `afterGroupMs`), the halves fade on top. Reduced motion / MOTION off: no sliding, no moving streak (the note splits in place and fades), poses change without the lunge. A wrong answer shakes the scroll (not under reduced motion). The results hero is the Sensei ('happy' with stars, else 'calm', in the belt). The staff stays large and still. `A.Ninja.sensei()` (the pose) for tests: tests/note-ninja-sensei.spec.js. (`A.ninjaSVG`, the old mascot, is still in ui.js; Note Ninja no longer shows it.)

## Chime Heist (`chime-heist/`)
- Mallet keyboard trainer with NO microphone (never load `pitch.js` or `mic-gate.js`). Always the `bells` group / `bells` member (written G3–C6, `sounds` −24): `player: 'bells'` in games.js; never touch `store.instId`. Loads `sfx.js`: `Sfx.bell(written + 24)` on every strike, right or wrong, plus the heist events.
- The bell kit: every bar is a `<button class="bar">` with `data-midi` (written), naturals below, accidentals raised in 2/3 groups, shorter as pitch rises; strikes on `pointerdown` (multi-touch safe, `touch-action: manipulation`); keyboard ←/→ along a row, ↑/↓ between rows, Enter/Space strike. Phones in portrait (≤ 599 px wide) get the "Turn your device sideways" screen; iPad portrait/landscape and 1366 × 768 must fit with no scrolling.
- NOTES × ORDER (`ModePicker` with `group`/`member` = bells), all saved under inst `bells`. Its old keys are kept: First 5 + Random `chime-heist`, scales in order `chime-heist:scale-<id>`, Chromatic + Scale Order `chime-heist:chromatic`, Chromatic + Random `chime-heist:full` (the old FULL RANGE button, now gone). Correct = the exact written midi (octave included; a same-letter bar in another octave is "Right note, wrong octave!" and a mistake). Scale notes' `midi` already includes the key signature.
- Vaults = levels in `chime-heist/levels.js` (`HEIST_VAULTS`: name, treasure, count, time, labels `all|faded|c|none`, onScreen for read-ahead, alarm segments, pool). Scoring/stars in `HEIST_RULES`. Mistakes and timeouts each add an alarm segment; a full meter = caught = 0 stars. Keep it a playful cartoon heist: no weapons, nobody hurt.

## Ancient Ninja Scrolls (`ancient-ninja-scrolls/`)
- THE UI KIT: the kit pause runs during Train / Spar / Review / the Belt Exam (BACK TO THE CHAMBER / TEMPLE; no RESTART in the exam); results = `UI.results` (Sensei hero); "Turn in your exam?" = `UI.confirm`; a scroll unrolled = `UI.toast`; the graded exam paper stays inline (TAKE IT AGAIN / CHAMBER). While a round or the exam runs, a plain strip as tall as the pause button's row covers the top of the screen (style.css `html.ui-in-level body::before`, `--an-top` from game.js `pauseGutter`), so the page scrolls under the strip, never under the button, and the sticky word bank sticks just below it.
- Band Ninja vocabulary trainer, Ranks 3–10. NO microphone and NO instrument (never load `pitch.js`/`mic-gate.js`; `player: 'all'`). Loads `belts.js` and `sfx.js`.
- `vocab.js` is Mat's source for the vocab tests: `VOCAB_BANKS` (word banks exactly as printed), `VOCAB_PASS` (max missed: Orange 0, Green 2, Blue–Diamond 3), `VOCAB` items `{id, rank, prompt (with ___), answer, type 'word'|'symbol', show?, tip?}`, `SCROLL_RULES`. Never change an item's `id` (saved progress uses it); every word answer must equal a bank word exactly, accents included (Più, L'istesso). Keep 15 items per rank.
- Modes: Train (4 choices from the belt's bank, every other question flipped, misses come back until right twice; stars 1 = round, 2 = 90% first try, 3 = all 15 mastered), Spar (60 s, combo, best per belt), Belt Exam (paper replica: bank + blanks, symbol picks, confirm, pass rule, TEST READY badge presented by the Sensei), Scroll Review (belts with a mastered scroll, weighted by misses). A term is mastered after 3 right answers in any mode. Every belt is open.
- Saved: `setLevel('ancient-ninja-scrolls', 'all', belt 1–8, {stars, best})` for Train (best = first-try %); `store.gameData('ancient-ninja-scrolls')` = `{terms: {id: {c, m}}, spar: {rank: best}, exams: {rank: {best, passed}}, badges: {rank: true}}`. games.js `badge` makes the floor show "Test Ready: n belts".
- The Sensei is an original character (topknot, round glasses, long white beard, indigo robe): keep it friendly and not like any known character. Prompts and answers are Atkinson Hyperlegible (the bundled subset now includes Latin-1 accents).

## Button Masher (`button-masher/`)
- THE UI KIT: the kit pause freezes the match timer and every delayed step (taunt, ROUND, FIGHT!, counters, K.O.: a pausable `later()`); BACK TO RIVALS; results hero = your still fighter + the rival.
- Fingering / slide-position trainer as a neon versus fighting game. NO microphone (never load `pitch.js` or `mic-gate.js`); loads `sfx.js`. No NOTES × ORDER picker: its 8 rivals ARE the modes (first 3, first 5, the four concert scales from `Scales.build(member, id).up`, then all four mixed), all under the plain key `button-masher`.
- Uses the saved player (`requireInstrument` + `Arcade.currentMember()`). THE FIGHTER IS THE STUDENT'S AVATAR (`Arcade.AvatarFight.mount`, as tall as the old fighter, feet on the arena floor) holding their instrument: idle → FIGHT-IDLE, a right combo → STRIKE + the sound-wave blast from the instrument to the rival (`FX.mouth()`), a counter after a timeout → HIT, after a wrong combo → DIZZY, the counter that empties the energy → KO, the rival K.O.'d → VICTORY, time over → BOW; the results show a still VICTORY/BOW (`stillHTML`); the pet cheers combos and worries at counters. Pose timing only: the rules, damage and timers are unchanged. The HUD shows the avatar's chip + its name (`#youName`, `data-av-name`). NO AVATAR SAVED YET (`!store.avatar`): the generic fighter (`fighterSVG`, unchanged) + a "Make your avatar!" button (`AvatarBadge.edit`; saving switches to the avatar at once), the instrument's portrait and "You" in the HUD (nothing in the game makes an avatar by itself). The rival screen draws the fight poses in the background (cached). Progress is keyed by MEMBER: `setLevel('button-masher', member.id, rival 1–8, {stars, best})` (`byMember: true` in games.js). Percussion (`bells`) gets "Percussion: try Chime Heist!" instead of a diagram, in the game and on the floor (`noPlay`).
- `shared/fingerings.js` is THE fingering table (moved out of button-masher/: Button Masher, Arcade Quest's FINGERING challenge, Scale Trainer's fingering card + NOTE BY NOTE load it; Music Highway loads it only for its octave fit, and filling the table moved no song: all 416 song × instrument octaves were compared before/after). It covers EVERY NOTE of every member's GMEA chromatic range (instruments.js `chromatic`, bells excepted) AND of the high school ranges (scales.js `CHROMATIC_HS`) (tests/scale-trainer.spec.js checks both); the notes added for Scale Trainer are listed under "ADDED FOR SCALE TRAINER, PLEASE CHECK" and "ADDED FOR SCALE TRAINER HS, PLEASE CHECK" in its top comment (filling the HS ranges moved no Music Highway song: all 736 song × instrument maps compared before/after). Button Masher and Arcade Quest still draw only from their own note pools (`MASHER_FINGERINGS[memberId] = {diagram, notes: {'Bb4': [primary, alternates…]}}`): brass `'1-3'`, `'0'`, horn trigger `'T1-2'`, 4th valve `'4'`; trombone positions `[1, 6]`; woodwinds = the diagram's key ids separated by spaces (`'|'` ignored, `'1h'` = half-hole). A combo is right only when the pressed keys EXACTLY match one entry. To fix a fingering, edit that list (primary first); every note used by rivals 1–8 must be listed for every member (a missing note or unknown key id warns in the console). Its top comment lists the fingerings to verify against the 6th Grade Honor Band charts; keep that list current.
- `shared/diagrams.js` (+ `shared/diagrams.css`; `Arcade.Masher` = `Arcade.Fingerings`) draws each family (`flute`, `oboe`, `clarinet`, `sax`, `bassoon`, `trumpet`, `horn`, `euph`, `tuba`, `trombone`) as SVG with real tappable keys (pointerdown). Key ids are what fingerings.js uses: move keys freely, never rename ids; a note that needs a key the drawing lacks gets a NEW id (added for the full ranges: flute `C#` `C` footjoint rollers, oboe `RC#` `RC` and (high school) `LB` `LBb` (low B / B♭, left pinky), clarinet `Sl` = the C♯/G♯ sliver key, bassoon `LBb` `LB` `LC` `LD` low keys on the left thumb). tests/diagrams.spec.js checks that no key or caption on any diagram overlaps another or leaves the picture. Half-hole keys (`half: true`) cycle open → half → closed. Clarinet (bass clarinet too) and sax: the LEFT pinky keys sit ABOVE the body (between the throat / palm keys and the side keys, over the gap between fingers 3 and 4, their label above them), the right pinky keys below (tests/diagrams.spec.js).
- Rivals in `levels.js` (`MASHER_RIVALS`: name, look, colors, taunt, pool, notes, health, time, showName, hint); rules in `MASHER_RULES`. `MASHER_RULES.timeByFamily` multiplies every rival's `time` by the player's family (woodwind 1.5, brass 1: woodwinds have many more keys to find); the rival cards and the match timer both use `timeFor(V)`, never `V.time` directly. Stars: 3 = no mistakes (wrong or timeout), 2 = 1–2, 1 = won. Cartoon only: energy blasts, a "boing" counter, dizzy stars; no weapons or injury. The fighter and the rivals are original drawings in `game.js`.
- CHART view (rival screen only, never during a match) shows every note with its primary fingering drawn and alternates listed. The `#chart` overlay itself scrolls (wheel, trackpad, touch, and arrows/Page Up/Down/Space: it gets focus on open); its header (the instrument's name + a big CLOSE) is `position: sticky`; Esc closes; `A.lockScroll(true/false)` holds the page behind still and restores it exactly. Bassoon key 1 is a half-hole key: tap once = half, twice = closed. `A.Masher.game()` = the match in progress (tests).
- Fingerings Mr. Graham has verified are listed under VERIFIED BY MR. GRAHAM in fingerings.js (bassoon E♭3 = W 1 3); never change those without asking him.

## Neon Face-Off (`neon-face-off/`)
- THE UI KIT: the kit pause freezes the whole match (`later()` is a pausable clock: countdown beats, CPU reactions, the serve, celebrations; the puck); BACK TO SETUP; results: the winner's color (`theme: 'nfo-res w-p1|w-p2'`), stars vs CPU only, REMATCH / MATCH SETUP (+ NEXT RIVAL). `A.FaceOff.state()` adds `frozen`, `puckT0`, `timers`.
- Two-player air hockey played with instruments, one device, one microphone, turn-based. Player 1 cyan, Player 2 magenta (`--pink`). Player 2 is `store.opponent`: another member, or `'cpu'` (the ladder). Humans are shown as avatars with their names (`p.name`; `p.inst` = the instrument) and a 1P/2P marker (`.pmark`): Player 1 = the device's avatar, a human Player 2 = the GUEST avatar (`Avatar.guest()`, SURPRISE ME on the setup screen re-rolls it; it never replaces the device's own). CPU rivals keep their recolored instrument portraits. Landscape: P1 left, P2 right; portrait: P2 on top (its panel rotated 180° to face them), P1 at the bottom. The table is a canvas (requestAnimationFrame, cheap shadows/gradients, dpr ≤ 1.5); reduced motion = no trail, no shake, no pulse.
- MICROPHONE: only the active player's target counts. Each turn: `Pitch.setInstrument(group)`, their range (`setRange(member range)` for scale/chromatic pools, `null` for First 5), `ignoreCurrent()` (the other player's note still ringing never counts: pitch.js STALE SOUND); the wrong-note line "That's D. Look again!" = `p.seq.name(pc)` for the ACTIVE player (their written note, the key signature's spelling in a scale; tests/faceoff-notes.spec.js plays real tones through a tone microphone); a target never shares its concert pitch class with the note the other player just played. HIT → NOTE: the receiver's note appears AT ONCE after a hit (`.side.waiting`: the note panel dimmed, status "…"); the in-rally sounds (puck-hit-soft/-hard, puck-smash, your-turn) mute the detector at most `RULES.maxHitSuppressMs` (300 ms, echo included: `Sfx.event(name, {muteCap})`); the game loop's `goLiveIfReady` waits for the REAL end of the mute (`Pitch.isSuppressed`, never a fixed guess), then lights the panel (`.side.live`, one short glow), starts the reaction clock (`M.noteAt`) AND the puck's travel (`M.puck.hold` until then; crossing time = max(base × power factor, the receiver's window), so a long sound never shortens a window); the puck + reaction clock also freeze during any later mute. The CPU plays silently (it goes live the same way).
- COUNTDOWNS (`shared/countdown.js`, voicePrefix 'faceoff', `RULES.countdown`): CLASSIC 3-2-1-GO before the first serve (after the one-time smash tip on a device's first match, `gameData.smashTip`, `RULES.tipMs`), READY-GO before the serve after every point (after `RULES.celebrateMs` 1300 of goal celebration: about 2.2 s in all with a "Go!" file under 0.45 s), CLASSIC instead when either player is one point from winning; never between rally hits. From the goal to the "GO!" the mic is PAUSED (`Pitch.pauseListening`: nothing counts, no note shows, the faceoff-* voices are mic: false); the serve note appears and the mic listens as the "GO!" voice ends. The beat is drawn big on the rink (`#count`, `showCount`; kind classes `k-ready`/`k-go`), twice in portrait (the top one turned for Player 2). The setup screen shows the smash tip (`.smash-tip`). `A.FaceOff.state()` = `{state ('count'|'serve'|'travel'|'celebrate'|'over'), active, live, liveWait, countStyle, countLog, hold, T, score, paused, suppressedFor}` for tests.
- Notes: `Arcade.buildSequence` per player (their own member, NOTES × ORDER from their own picker, Rookie = the smaller level-1 pool). Strike power from reaction (note shown → note registered): `FACEOFF_RULES.power` (SMASH! / POWER / GOOD / WEAK) × a rally base that shrinks each return; a shot never gives the receiver less than their difficulty's window (+ `suppressMs`). A timeout = the other player scores; `serve: 'loser'` (the player scored on serves) or `'alternate'`. Wrong note: "That's D" in the player's written pitch, time keeps running.
- `levels.js`: `FACEOFF_RIVALS` (8 CPU rivals: name, color, plays (an instrument id for its portrait), reaction [min, max] s, accuracy), `FACEOFF_DIFFICULTY` (Rookie 4 s, Pro 2.5 s, All-Star 1.5 s), `FACEOFF_RULES`. Stars vs CPU: win 1, win by 4+ 2, shutout 3; saved `setLevel('neon-face-off', Player 1's member id, rival, {stars, best})` (`byMember`). Two-player matches: no stars; `store.gameData('neon-face-off')` = `{settings: {p1: {diff}, p2: {diff}, points, rival}, h2h: {'<p1 member>><p2 member>': {p1, p2}}, cpuWins}` (cpuWins: every win over the CPU, for the Air Rink background).
- `?demo`: every rival unlocked; hold Space = the active player's note (press later = slower), W = a wrong note.

## Showtime Malfunction (`showtime-malfunction/`)
- THE UI KIT: the kit pause uses its own flag `G.held` (never the special card's `G.paused`); scare, walk-home and fizzle timers run on a pausable show clock; the spooky level is also registered in the Settings panel; the Jump Scare warning is `UI.confirm` (with the "Visual scares only" checkbox).
- Articulation game, LISTENS (pitch.js + mic-gate.js + sfx.js): glitching animatronics lurch toward the student in 2–3 lanes (pseudo-3D DOM: translate + scale from the feet, stop-motion lurch steps every `lurchMs`); each shows a VOICE BOX (the item's `show` on the student's clef with `sig`, and × N). The target = the closest walking one. THE TARGET PANEL (`#tpanel`, `drawPanel`) always shows the target's note BIG (one note on the student's clef with `sig`; the viewBox fits the whole pool so the staff never jumps, except Chromatic, where each note gets its own box; no note name) and "× N" in huge numbers, redrawn at once when the target or the Maestro's phase changes (a short flash/slide; a color fade under reduced motion); snare = a big drum icon + count. SNARE players face the same band, special machines and Maestro Moose as everyone else (only the counting differs: hits; `howSnare` lines and the Lurker's roll). It sits BESIDE the arena in landscape and BELOW it in portrait and on phones (≤ 760 px), outside the arena, so static, vignette, emergency light and lean-in never cover or dim it; the arena shrinks to make room. The target gets a yellow outline, floor ring and a dashed tether to the panel's side; the next one's sign says NEXT. Signs are previews: tight single-note staffs that never shrink below 0.8× (0.7 on phones), slide down instead of leaving the top of the arena, dark notation on a light card, glitches only on the frame (`.sign::before`). Every staff in this game has thicker lines, stems, ledger lines and outlined note heads (style.css). `Pitch.onAttack`: pc === item.pc counts one down (snare: any attack); wrong pc = a `sour` glitch; a note held > `holdHintMs` with no attack = "Tongue each note!". 0 = reboot (eyes blue `.fixed`, walks back, joins `#band` on the stage). THE LINEUP (`lineup(box, {maxH})` in game.js, `LINEUP`): the stage band and the results band always fit, however many (up to 10 + 20 Split Sprocket minis + the Maestro): overlapping, smaller and up to 4 staggered rows (the front row on top), Maestro Moose in the middle of the front row; redone on resize. The results show it BELOW the stars (`onShow`, `#resBand`, at most `resBandMax()` tall). Reaching the front = a spotlight out; 3 out = SHOWTIME'S OVER (0 stars). Stars 3/2/1 = spotlights lost 0/1/2. The band freezes while `Pitch.isSuppressed()`.
- `levels.js` = `SHOWTIMES` (bots, count, snare counts, atOnce, lanes, walk s, pool, boss {count, phases, snare, walk}) + `SHOWTIME_RULES`. Showtime 8 = Maestro Moose in phases (a new note each phase, staggers back). Notes ONLY from `ModePicker.sequence` (count = bots + boss phases).
- THE SNARE DRUM'S JOBS (`showtime-malfunction/snare.js` = `Arcade.ShowtimeSnare`, created by game.js ONLY when `snare`; every number in levels.js `SNARE_RULES`, one commented table, each feature a `from` showtime + per-showtime arrays; wind players never touch any of it, and their rules and tests are unchanged). THE MICROPHONE HEARS ONLY WHEN A HIT LANDS AND HOW LOUD IT IS: it can't hear which hand played, so NEVER judge sticking. Hits = `Arcade.Onsets.listen` (never pitch; `onAttack` is ignored for the snare), each hit's time minus the device's saved delay (`Calibration.lag('clap')`: Rhythm Dojo's timing check, shared; the snare card's "Check your timing" runs the same `TimingCheck` flow). THE SNARE CLOCK `H.clk` = real time added up only while nothing holds the show (suppression, PAUSE `G.held`, a card `G.paused`, a scare, a hidden tab: `still()`); any still frame during a rhythm/accent/tempo job restarts it from its count-in. THE SILENT BEAT: no clicks or metronome during play; the stage and the target panel's border swell on each beat (`--pulse`, ≤ `beat.maxSwells` 2.2 a second, steady under reduced motion) + beat dots on the panel.
